\displaystyle-\frac{\tilde{L}}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{D}{L}}\Big(\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{2\tilde{L}}+\frac{L}{L-\tilde{L}}\Big)E_{1}(y_{N}(0))-\tilde{L}\sqrt{\frac{D}{2L}}\Big(\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{2\tilde{L}}+\frac{L}{L-\tilde{L}}\Big)E_{2}(y_{N}(0)).

Let

c_{1}:=\left(\frac{1}{2D}-\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{\tilde{L}\sqrt{2DL}}\right),\;c_{2}:=\frac{1}{2\delta}\left(\frac{1}{2D}-\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{2\tilde{L}\sqrt{DL}}\right).

(3.33)

Recalling assumption (
1.8
), we can verify that
c_{1}>0
,
c_{2}>0
. It is possible to choose
\delta
small enough such that

\delta\Big(\frac{\|\Delta\chi\|_{L^{\infty}}}{4L}+\frac{(L-\tilde{L})}{2L\tilde{L}}\sqrt{\frac{D}{2L}}\|\nabla\chi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big)\leq\frac{c_{1}}{2}.

(3.34)

Subsequently, if

\displaystyle c_{1}E_{2}(v_{N}(T))+c_{2}E_{1}(v_{N}(T))

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{L}}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{D}{L}}\Big(\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{2\tilde{L}}+\frac{L}{L-\tilde{L}}\Big)E_{1}(y_{N}(0))+\tilde{L}\sqrt{\frac{D}{2L}}\Big(\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{2\tilde{L}}+\frac{L}{L-\tilde{L}}\Big)E_{2}(y_{N}(0))

\displaystyle=:~

\displaystyle d_{1}E_{1}(y_{N}(0))+d_{2}E_{2}(y_{N}(0))

(3.35)

is valid, we can derive

\displaystyle(B_{N}\vec{l},A_{N}F_{N}(x_{0}))_{\tilde{l}_{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\partial_{t}y_{N}(T)\partial_{t}v_{N}(T)+\nabla y_{N}(T)\cdot\nabla v_{N}(T)\Big){\mathrm{d}}x

(3.36)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\Big(\nabla\partial_{t}y_{N}(T)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}v_{N}(T)+\Delta y_{N}(T)\Delta v_{N}(T)\Big){\mathrm{d}}x\geq 0.

Since
\{\varphi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is the standard orthogonal basis of
L^{2}(\Omega)
satisfying (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle c_{1}E_{2}(v_{N}(T))+c_{2}E_{1}(v_{N}(T))

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left((c_{1}\lambda_{i}^{4}+c_{2}\lambda_{i}^{2})|a_{i}|^{2}+(c_{1}\lambda_{i}^{2}+c_{2})|b_{i}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle\min\{c_{1},c_{2}\}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\lambda_{i}^{4}|a_{i}|^{2}+\lambda_{i}^{2}|b_{i}|^{2}\right)=\min\{c_{1},c_{2}\}|\Lambda_{N}\vec{l}|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}.

(3.37)

Recalling the initial energy upper bound condition (
3.9
), we then have

d_{1}E_{1}(y_{N}(0))+d_{2}E_{2}(y_{N}(0))\leq\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}(E_{1}(y(0))+E_{2}(y(0))).

(3.38)

Hence, we define

R:=\sqrt{\frac{\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}(E_{1}(y(0))+E_{2}(y(0)))}{\min\{c_{1},c_{2}\}}},

(3.39)

and therefore if

|\Lambda_{N}\vec{l}|_{\ell_{2}}\geq R,

then (
3.15
) holds. Moreover,
R
is independent of
N
.
∎

Now we are in a position to prove Theorem
1.1
.

Proof..

For any
N>0
, by Lemma
3.1
, there exists a
\vec{l}_{N}=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{N},b_{1},\cdots,b_{N})
satisfying

\mathcal{F}_{N}(\vec{l}_{N})=0
. Thanks to the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{N}
, this indeed implies that
(y_{N}(T),y_{Nt}(T))=(0,0)
. Then we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\partial_{t}y_{N}(T)\partial_{t}v_{N}(T)+\nabla y_{N}(T)\cdot\nabla v_{N}(T)\Big){\mathrm{d}}x

(3.40)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\Big(\nabla\partial_{t}y_{N}(T)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}v_{N}(T)+\Delta y_{N}(T)\Delta v_{N}(T)\Big){\mathrm{d}}x=0.

Thus, referring to (
3
), we find that

\displaystyle\delta\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\big|\nabla\partial_{t}v_{N}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}|\partial_{t}v_{N}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C^{*}\left(E_{0}(y_{N}(0))+\delta E_{1}(y_{N}(0))\right)

(3.41)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C^{*}\left(E_{0}(y(0))+\delta E_{1}(y(0))\right),

where the constant is given by

\displaystyle C^{*}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{D}{L}}\Big(\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{2}+\frac{L\tilde{L}}{L-\tilde{L}}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{L-\tilde{L}}{\tilde{L}}\sqrt{\frac{2D}{L}}\Big)^{-1}

(3.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{L^{2}+\tilde{L}^{2}}{2(L-\tilde{L})}\frac{\tilde{L}\sqrt{D}}{\tilde{L}\sqrt{L}-(L-\tilde{L})\sqrt{2D}}.

It follows from (
3
) that
\{\partial_{t}v_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})
, and hence there exists a subsequence that converges weakly. Furthermore, by the energy estimates (
3.19
) and (
3.22
),

\displaystyle\{y_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega)\cap H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)),

(3.43)

\displaystyle\big\{\partial_{t}y_{N}\big\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\big\{f\big(\partial_{t}y_{N}\big)\big\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)),

are bounded sequences. From the system of
y_{N}
, (
3.43
) infers
\{\partial_{t}^{2}y_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
. Therefore, we can extract a subsequence of
\{y_{N}\}
(still denoted as
\{y_{N}\}
), such that there exist
y\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}\cap H_{0}^{1})
,
z\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{0}^{1})
, and

\begin{cases}y_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\longrightarrow}}y,&\quad\text{in}~L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega)\cap H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)),\\
\partial_{t}y_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\longrightarrow}}y_{t},&\quad\text{in}~L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)),\\
\partial_{t}^{2}y_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\longrightarrow}}y_{tt},&\quad\text{in}~L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),\\
f\big(\partial_{t}y_{N}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\longrightarrow}}z,&\quad\text{in}~L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)).\end{cases}

(3.44)

On the other hand, by a compactness argument (refer to
[
31
]
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}y_{N}\longrightarrow y_{t},\quad\text{in}~L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

(3.45)

and

\begin{cases}y_{N}(T)\longrightarrow y(T),\quad\text{in}~\mathcal{H}^{1},\\
\partial_{t}y_{N}(T)\longrightarrow y_{t}(T),\quad\text{in}~L^{2}(\Omega).\end{cases}

(3.46)

Thus, given that
f
is a Lipschitz function,

\displaystyle f\big(\partial_{t}y_{N}\big)\longrightarrow f(y_{t}),\quad\text{in}~L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)).

By the uniqueness of the limit, we conclude that
z=f(y_{t})
, implying

\displaystyle f\big(\partial_{t}y_{N}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\longrightarrow}}f(y_{t}),

\displaystyle\quad\text{in}~L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)).

Consequently, the approximation solutions
\{y_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
converge to a weak solution
y\in C^{0}((0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1})\cap C^{1}((0,T];L^{2})
of (
1.5
) in the sense of
L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
. Moreover, the weak limit
u
of
\{\partial_{t}v_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is the desired control function. Letting
N\to\infty
in (
3
), we obtain (
1.9
) with
D^{*}=\frac{C^{*}}{\delta}
, where
\delta
and
C^{*}
are defined in (
3.34
) and (
3.42
).
∎

4
Proof of Theorem
1.2

This section is devoted to proving Theorem
1.2
. As mentioned in the introduction part, it is sufficient to consider the null controllability problem for quasi-linear damped wave equation

\left\{\begin{aligned} &y_{tt}+b_{0}y_{t}-{\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}y_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}}+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}y_{x_{k}}+\tilde{b}y=\chi_{\omega}u,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&y(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&y(0,x)=y^{0},~y_{t}(0,x)=y^{1},&x\in&~\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.1)

with

\begin{cases}a_{ij}=a_{ji}=\delta_{ij}-g^{ij}_{2}(t,x,y,y_{t},\nabla y),\quad i,j=1,\cdots,n,\\
b_{0}=b_{0}(t,x,y,y_{t},\nabla y)=1+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial g_{1}}{\partial y_{t}}(t,x,\tau y,\tau y_{t},\tau\nabla y){\mathrm{d}}\tau,\\
b_{k}=b_{k}(t,x,y,y_{t},\nabla y)=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial g_{1}}{\partial y_{x_{k}}}(t,x,\tau y,\tau y_{t},\tau\nabla y){\mathrm{d}}\tau,\quad k=1,\cdots,n\\
\tilde{b}=\tilde{b}(t,x,y,y_{t},\nabla y)=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial g_{1}}{\partial y}(t,x,\tau y,\tau y_{t},\tau\nabla y){\mathrm{d}}\tau.\end{cases}

(4.2)

4.1
Existence of solutions of system (
4.1
)

Considering the damping term
y_{t}
, we aim to develop an algorithmic framework that not only establishes the existence of the solution to (
4.1
) but also achieves null controllability for the system described by (
4.1
). We start by focusing on the linearized version of the system (
4.1
). To this end, we introduce the following iterative procedure: We initialize with
(z^{(0)},v^{(0)})\equiv(0,0)
. For each
\alpha\geq 1
, given the previous iteration
(z^{(\alpha-1)},v^{(\alpha-1)})
, we define the next iteration
(z^{(\alpha)},v^{(\alpha)})
as detailed below

\left\{\begin{aligned} &z^{(\alpha)}_{tt}-b^{(\alpha)}_{0}z^{(\alpha)}_{t}-{\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)}&&\\
&~+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&z^{(\alpha)}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&z^{(\alpha)}(T,x)=v^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x)+z^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x),&x\in&~\Omega,\\
&z^{(\alpha)}_{t}(T,x)=v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x)+z_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x),&x\in&~\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.3)

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} &v^{(\alpha)}_{tt}+b^{(\alpha)}_{0}v^{(\alpha)}_{t}-{\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}v^{(\alpha)}&&\\
&~+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b^{(\alpha)}_{i}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}=-2\chi\cdot z^{(\alpha)}_{t},&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&v^{(\alpha)}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&v^{(\alpha)}(0,x)=y^{0},~v^{(\alpha)}_{t}(0,x)=y^{1},&x\in&~\Omega\end{aligned}\right.

(4.4)

where

\begin{cases}&a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}=a_{ij}(t,x,v^{(\alpha-1)},v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla v^{(\alpha-1)}),\quad i,j=1,\cdots,n,\\
&b^{(\alpha)}_{i}=b_{i}(t,x,v^{(\alpha-1)},v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla v^{(\alpha-1)}),\quad i=0,\cdots,n,\\
&\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}=\tilde{b}(t,x,v^{(\alpha-1)},v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla v^{(\alpha-1)}).\end{cases}

(4.5)

We provide some remarks on the assumptions made on the coefficients.

Remark 4.1
.

Thanks to the assumptions on
g_{2}^{ij}
and
g_{1}
, we have the following relations: as
|y|+|\nabla y|+|y_{t}|\rightarrow 0
, for any
(t,x)\in\overline{(0,T)\times\Omega}
,

\displaystyle a_{ij}=~

\displaystyle\delta_{ij}+O(|y|+|\nabla y|+|y_{t}|),\quad b_{0}=1+O(|y|+|\nabla y|+|y_{t}|),

\displaystyle b_{k}=~

\displaystyle O(|y|+|\nabla y|+|y_{t}|),\quad\tilde{b}=O(|y|+|\nabla y|+|y_{t}|).

(4.6)

Remark 4.2
.

With the help of (
4.1
), the recurrence relation (
4.5
) can be equivalently written as

\begin{split}a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}&=\delta_{ij}+a_{ij,0}v^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}+a_{ij,k}v^{(\alpha-1)}_{x_{k}}+a_{ij,n+1}v^{(\alpha-1)},i,j,k=1,\cdots,n\\
b_{0}^{(\alpha)}&=1+b_{0,0}v^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}+b_{0,i}v^{(\alpha-1)}_{x_{i}}+b_{0,n+1}v^{(\alpha-1)},i=1,\cdots,n\\
b_{k}^{(\alpha)}&=b_{k,0}v^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}+a_{k,i}v^{(\alpha-1)}_{x_{i}}+b_{k,n+1}v^{(\alpha-1)},k,i=1,\cdots,n;\\
\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}&=\tilde{b}_{0}v^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}+\tilde{b}_{i}v_{x_{i}}^{(\alpha-1)}+\tilde{b}_{n+1}v^{(\alpha-1)},k,i=1,\cdots,n\\
\end{split}

(4.7)

where
a_{ij,k},i,j=1,\cdots,n,k=0,1,\cdots,n+1
,
b_{i,k},i,k=0,1,\cdots,n+1
and
\tilde{b}_{i},i=0,1,\cdots,n+1
are smooth bounded functions.

Now we state the following proposition:

Proposition 4.1
.

Let the sequences
v^{(\alpha)}
and
z^{(\alpha)}
be the solutions of (
4.4
) and (
4.3
), respectively. Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
1.2
, there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{prop}>0
, such that the norm condition for initial data
(y^{0},y^{1})
is satisfied:

\|y^{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|y^{1}\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq\varepsilon_{prop},

where
s\geq\max\{n+2,4\}
.
Then for any
t\in[0,T]
, we have that as
\alpha\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle(v^{(\alpha)}(t),v^{(\alpha)}_{t}(t),v^{(\alpha)}_{tt}(t))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\big(y(t),y_{t}(t),y_{tt}(t)\big),\quad\text{in}~\mathcal{H}^{s-1}\times\mathcal{H}^{s-2}\times\mathcal{H}^{s-3},

\displaystyle(z^{(\alpha)}_{t}(t),z^{(\alpha)}_{tt}(t))

\displaystyle\rightarrow(u(t),u_{t}(t)),\quad\text{in}~\mathcal{H}^{s-2}\times\mathcal{H}^{s-3},

where limit functions
y\in\cap_{p=0}^{2}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-p})
and
u\in\cap_{p=0}^{1}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p})
are solutions to the quasilinear system (
4.1
), subject to the terminal conditions

(y(T),y_{t}(T))=(0,0).

Notice that the existence part in Theorem
1.2
follows from Proposition
4.1
directly.

In order to obtain the convergence properties of sequences
v^{(\alpha)}
and
z^{(\alpha)}
, we need to estimate the error of iteration
(v^{(\alpha)},z^{(\alpha)})
, thus,
we define

V^{(\alpha)}=v^{(\alpha)}-v^{(\alpha-1)},~Z^{(\alpha)}=z^{(\alpha)}-z^{(\alpha-1)},

(4.8)

Then we get
V^{(1)}=v^{(1)},Z^{(1)}=z^{(1)}
and for each
\alpha\geq 2
, sequence
V^{(\alpha)}
and
Z^{(\alpha)}
solve the equations

\left\{\begin{aligned} &V^{(\alpha)}_{tt}+b_{0}^{(\alpha)}V^{(\alpha)}_{t}-{\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}V^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}V^{(\alpha)}&&\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}V^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}=F^{(\alpha)}-2\chi\cdot Z_{t}^{(\alpha)},&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&V^{(\alpha)}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&V^{(\alpha)}(0,x)=0,~V^{(\alpha)}_{t}(0,x)=0,&x\in&~\Omega\end{aligned}\right.

(4.9)

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} &Z^{(\alpha)}_{tt}-b_{0}^{(\alpha)}Z^{(\alpha)}_{t}-{\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}Z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}Z^{(\alpha)}&&\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}Z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}=H^{(\alpha)},&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&Z^{(\alpha)}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&Z^{(\alpha)}(T,x)=v^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x),~Z^{(\alpha)}_{t}(T,x)=v^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}(T,x)&x\in&~\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.10)

where

\displaystyle F^{(\alpha)}=

\displaystyle\big(b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-b_{0}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)v^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big[\big(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}-a_{ij}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)v^{(\alpha-1)}_{x_{i}}\big]_{x_{j}}

(4.11)

\displaystyle+\big(\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}-\tilde{b}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)v^{(\alpha-1)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\big(b_{i}^{(\alpha)}-b_{i}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)v^{(\alpha-1)}_{x_{i}},

and

\displaystyle H^{(\alpha)}=

\displaystyle-\big(b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-b_{0}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)z^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big[\big(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}-a_{ij}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)z^{(\alpha-1)}_{x_{i}}\big]_{x_{j}}

(4.12)

\displaystyle+\big(\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}-\tilde{b}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)z^{(\alpha-1)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\big(b_{i}^{(\alpha)}-b_{i}^{(\alpha-1)}\big)z^{(\alpha-1)}_{i}.

The key of the proof of Proposition
4.1
is the following estimates:

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
s\geq\max\{n+2,4\}
be an integer. There exists a small
\varepsilon_{lem}>0
and
0<\delta<1
, such that for each
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{lem}
and for all
\alpha\geq 1
, System (
4.3
)–(
4.4
) admits a unique solution
(v^{(\alpha)},z^{(\alpha)})
with initial data holding

\|y^{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|y^{1}\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq\varepsilon.

(4.13)

Moreover, for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\alpha\geq 1
. The sequences
(v^{(\alpha)},z^{(\alpha)})
and
(V^{(\alpha)},Z^{(\alpha)})
satisfy

\displaystyle\|V^{(\alpha)}\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|V^{(\alpha)}_{t}(t)\|_{H^{s-2}}^{2}\leq(1-\delta)^{2\alpha}{C_{V,s}}\varepsilon^{2},

(4.14)

\displaystyle\|Z^{(\alpha)}(t)\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|Z^{(\alpha)}_{t}(t)\|_{H^{s-2}}^{2}\leq(1-\delta)^{2\alpha}C_{Z,s}\varepsilon^{2},

and

\displaystyle\big\|v^{(\alpha)}(t)\big\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\big\|v^{(\alpha)}_{t}(t)\big\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\big\|v^{(\alpha)}_{tt}(t)\big\|_{H^{s-2}}^{2}\leq C_{v,s}\varepsilon^{2},

(4.15)

\displaystyle\big\|z^{(\alpha)}(t)\big\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\big\|z^{(\alpha)}_{t}(t)\big\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\big\|v^{(\alpha)}_{tt}(t)\big\|_{H^{s-2}}^{2}\leq C_{z,s}\varepsilon^{2},

where
C_{V,s},C_{Z,s},C_{v,s},C_{z,s}
are positive constants independent of
\alpha,\varepsilon
.

The demonstration of Lemma
4.2
is long, hence we postpone the proof in the next subsection. We give the proof of Proposition
4.1
when assuming Lemma
4.2
holds.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
assuming Lemma
4.2
holds.

By the definition of
V^{(\alpha)}
given in (
4.8
), equation (
4.14
) entails that

\left\|v^{(\alpha)}-v^{(\beta)}\right\|_{C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1})}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=\beta}^{\alpha}\left\|V^{(i)}\right\|_{C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1})}^{2}\leq\frac{(1-\delta)^{2\beta}}{1-(1-\delta)^{2}}(C_{V,s-1})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}.

(4.16)

Since
0<\delta<1
, inequality (
4.16
) and (
4.15
) with
k=s-1
indicate that for each
t\in[0,T]
, the sequence
\{v^{(\alpha)}(t)\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}
constitutes a Cauchy sequence in
\mathcal{H}^{s-1}
. Thus, this together with
\{v_{t}^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1})
implies that there exists
y\in C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1})
such that
\{v^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}
converges strongly to
y
in
C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1})
.

By utilizing (
4.14
) and (
4.15
), we can also deduce that
\{v_{t}^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1})
and
\{v_{tt}^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2})
converge strongly to
\tilde{v}_{1}\in C(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2})
and
\tilde{v}_{2}\in C(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-3})
, respectively. Moreover, by (
4.15
), we know
\{v_{tt}^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2})
and
\{v_{ttt}^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}\subset L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-3})
, so according to compactness argument, we have
\tilde{v}_{1}=y_{t}\in C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2})
and
\tilde{v}_{2}=y_{tt}\in C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-3})
.

Similarly, we can establish the existence of
z\in C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1}),z_{t}\in C^{0}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2})
.

Noting that
s\geq\max\{n+2,4\}\geq\frac{n}{2}+3
, hence by Morrey’s embedding inequality,
\mathcal{H}^{s-2}\subset C^{1}(\Omega)
. This implies that for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{cases}&a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}(t,x,v^{(\alpha)},v^{(\alpha)}_{t},\nabla v^{(\alpha)})\rightarrow a_{ij}(t,x,y,y_{t},\nabla y),\quad i,j=1,\cdots,n,\\
&b^{(\alpha)}_{i}(t,x,v^{(\alpha)},v^{(\alpha)}_{t},\nabla v^{(\alpha)})\rightarrow b_{i}(t,x,y,y_{t},\nabla y),\quad i=0,\cdots,n,\\
&\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}(t,x,v^{(\alpha)},v^{(\alpha)}_{t},\nabla v^{(\alpha)})\rightarrow\tilde{b}(t,x,y,y_{t}y,\nabla y).\end{cases}

(4.17)

in
C^{1}(\Omega)
, as
\alpha
goes to
\infty
.

By the way, we note that for both initial and terminal values satisfy

(v^{(\alpha)}(0),v_{t}^{(\alpha)}(0))\rightarrow(y(0),y_{t}(0)),~\text{in}\quad\mathcal{H}^{s-1}\times\mathcal{H}^{s-2},

(4.18)

and

(z^{(\alpha)}(T),z_{t}^{(\alpha)}(T))\rightarrow(z(T),z_{t}(T)),~\text{in}\quad\mathcal{H}^{s-1}\times\mathcal{H}^{s-2}.

(4.19)

Given the initial condition of system (
4.4
) and the terminal condition of system (
4.3
), letting
\alpha
goes to
\infty
, we obtain

\begin{split}(y(0),y_{t}(0))=&(\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}v^{(\alpha)}(0),\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}v^{(\alpha)}(0))=(y^{0},y^{1}),\\
(y(T),y_{t}(T))=&(\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}v^{(\alpha)}(T),\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}v^{(\alpha)}(T)),\\
(\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}z^{(\alpha)}(T),\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}z_{t}^{(\alpha)}(T))=&(\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}z^{(\alpha-1)}(T),\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}z_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}(T))\\
&+(\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}v^{(\alpha-1)}(T),\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}v^{(\alpha-1)}(T)).\end{split}

(4.20)

This immediately implies that in
\mathcal{H}^{s-1}\times\mathcal{H}^{s-2}

(y(0),y_{t}(0))=(y^{0},y^{1}),(y(T),y_{t}(T))=(0,0).

(4.21)

Next, from (
4.15
) and the compactness argument, we can deduce that there exists a subsequence of
\{v^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha=1}^{\infty}
(denoted as
\{v^{(\alpha_{1})}\}_{\alpha_{1}=1}^{\infty}
) and
\tilde{y}\in\cap_{p=0}^{2}W^{p,\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-p})
, such that

(v^{(\alpha_{1})},v_{t}^{(\alpha_{1})})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\tilde{y},\tilde{y}_{t}),~\text{in }~L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s})\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1}),

(4.22)

as
\alpha_{1}
goes to
\infty
.

Since the limit is unique, we conclude that
y=\tilde{y}\in\cap_{p=0}^{2}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-p})
.
By analogous reasoning, we can establish that
\{z^{(\alpha)}\}
converges strongly to
z\in\cap_{p=0}^{2}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-p})
.

Finally, letting
u=-2z_{t}
, these convergence results imply that
(y,u)
is a solution of System (
4.1
) with initial data
(y^{0},y^{1})

and satisfying the terminal conditions

(y(T),y_{t}(T))=(0,0)
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.2
Proof of Lemma
4.2

The proof of Lemma
4.2
consists of two points. The first one is to prove the well-posedness of the system (
4.3
), (
4.4
), (
4.9
), (
4.10
) for each
\alpha
. The second one is to show that the corresponding solutions satisfy the estimates (
4.14
) and (
4.15
).

Before we state the well-posedness results for System (
4.3
)–(
4.4
), it is imperative to establish a norm bound for composite functions. This estimation is essential for the subsequent analysis of the coefficients within our iterative scheme.

We can have the following conclusion from the preceding discussion.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
s\geq n+2
be an integer.
Assume that there exists a constant
\nu_{1}
such that

\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|\partial_{t}^{p}z^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}}+\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}(z^{(\alpha-1)}-z^{(\alpha-2)})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}\leq\nu_{1},

(4.23)

then we have for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}\sum_{p=0}^{s-2}\|\partial_{t}^{p}F^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-2-p}}\leq C_{F}\big(\sum_{p=0}^{s-2}\|\partial_{t}^{p}(v^{(\alpha-1)}-v^{(\alpha-2)})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}\big)\big(\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|\partial_{t}^{p}v^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}}\big),\\
\sum_{p=0}^{s-2}\|\partial_{t}^{p}H^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-2-p}}\leq C_{H}\big(\sum_{p=0}^{s-2}\|\partial_{t}^{p}(v^{(\alpha-1)}-v^{(\alpha-2)})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}\big)\big(\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|\partial_{t}^{p}z^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}}\big),\end{split}

(4.24)

for some constants
C_{F},C_{H}
depending on
\nu_{1},s,T,n
and the bounds of
a_{ij,k},i,j=1,\cdots,n,k=0,1,\cdots,n+1
,
b_{i,k},i,k=0,1,\cdots,n+1
and
\tilde{b}_{i},i=0,1,\cdots,n+1
in Remark
4.2
independent of
\alpha
.

Here, we present a lemma on the norm estimate of a composite function in a bounded domain, which can be referred to as
[
21
, Lemma 2.1]
. This lemma plays a crucial role in estimating the coefficients in the subsequent iterative estimates.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
l>n
be an integer and
T>0
.
Let
G(t,\gamma)=G(t,\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{M})\in C^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{M})
be a bounded smooth function and satisfies

\|G\|_{C^{l}([0,T]\times\Omega)}\leq C_{\Omega},

(4.25)

for some constant
C_{\Omega}
depends on
T
and
\Omega
.
If there exists a small positive constant
\nu_{1}
, such that

\sum_{p=0}^{l}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}}\leq\nu_{1},i=1,\cdots,M,

(4.26)

then for any
t\in[0,T]
, for any
u,v\in\cap_{p=0}^{l}C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{l-p})
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{p=0}^{l}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}G(\gamma)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-p}}&\leq C_{1},\\
\sum_{p=0}^{l}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(G(\gamma)u)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}}&\leq C_{2}(\sum_{p=0}^{l}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-p}}),\\
\sum_{p=0}^{l}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(G(\gamma)uv)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}}&\leq C_{3}(\sum_{p=0}^{l}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}})(\sum_{p=0}^{l}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}v\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}}),\end{split}

(4.27)

where
C_{i}=C_{i}(n,C_{\Omega},M,T,\nu_{1},s)>0,i=1,2,3
depend on
n,C_{\Omega},M,T,\nu_{1}
and
s
.

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

Thanks to (
4.25
) and (
4.26
), the first inequality is straightforward. Moreover, since
l-\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor>\frac{n}{2}
, the Morrey inequality implies that for any
t\in[0,T]
and
k\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor
,

\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}G(\gamma)\big\|_{C(\Omega)}\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}G(\gamma)\big\|_{H^{l-\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}}\leq\tilde{C}_{G},

(4.28)

for some constant
\tilde{C}_{G}=\tilde{C}_{G}(n,M,C_{\Omega},T,\nu_{1},l)
.

Next, we focus on verifying the second inequality in (
4.27
), as the remaining ones can be proven similarly. Let

\partial_{x}=\{\partial_{x_{1}},\cdots,\partial_{x_{n}}\}
. Given two multi-index

L,K
with
|L|+|K|=l
, following the method in
[
21
, Lemma 2.1]
, we directly compute

\partial_{t}^{L}\partial_{x}^{K}\big[G(\gamma)u\big]=\sum\limits_{|K_{1}|+|K_{2}|=|K|}\big[\sum\limits_{|L_{1}|+|L_{2}|=|L|}C_{K_{1},K_{2},L_{1},L_{2}}\partial_{t}^{L_{1}}\partial_{x}^{K_{1}}G(\gamma)\partial_{t}^{L_{2}}\partial_{x}^{K_{2}}u\big],

(4.29)

where

\partial_{x}^{K_{1}}G(\gamma)=\sum\limits_{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{M}l_{j}=l,1\leq m\leq|K_{1}|}\frac{\partial^{m}_{x}G}{\partial_{x}^{l_{1}}\gamma_{1}\cdot\cdot\cdot\partial^{l_{M}}\gamma_{M}}(\partial_{x}\gamma)^{a_{1}}\cdot(\partial_{x}^{|K_{1}|}\gamma)^{a_{|K_{1}|}},

(4.30)

with
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{|K_{1}|}|a_{j}|=l
and
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{|K_{1}|}|j||a_{j}|=|K_{1}|
, and
C_{K_{1},K_{2},L_{1},L_{2}}
are constants independent of
G
and
u
.

Next, we divide the terms on the right-hand side of (
4.29
) into two parts based on
|L_{1}|+|L_{2}|\leq l
. The first part is when
|L_{2}|\geq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor
which implies
|L_{1}|\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor
. Combining this with (
4.28
) and taking the
L^{2}
norm of these terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\limits_{|K_{1}|+|K_{2}|=|K|}\big[\sum\limits_{|L_{1}|+|L_{2}|=|L|,|L_{1}|\leq\frac{n}{2}}C_{K_{1},K_{2},L_{1},L_{2}}\partial_{t}^{L_{1}}\partial_{x}^{K_{1}}G(\gamma)\partial_{t}^{L_{2}}\partial_{x}^{K_{2}}u\big]\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C\|u\|_{\cap_{k=0}^{l}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-k})}

for some constant
C
.
The second part is when
|L_{2}|\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor
, which implies that
\partial_{t}^{|L_{2}|}u\in C(0,T;H^{l-|L_{2}|})
and
l-|L_{2}|>\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor
.
Then we observe that for any
t\in[0,T]
, the Sobolev space
H^{l-|L_{2}|}
is a Banach algebra. Thus, we have

\|(\partial\gamma)^{a_{1}}\cdots(\partial^{|K_{1}|}\gamma)^{a_{|K_{1}|}}\partial^{K_{2}}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\gamma\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})}^{|K_{1}|}\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})}.

(4.31)

Here we also use the fact that
\gamma,u\in\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})\subset\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;H^{l-p})
and for any
t\in[0,T]
,
\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})}\sim\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;H^{l-p})}.
*
*

*

Here we say that
\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})}\sim\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;H^{l-p})},
for any
u\in\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})
, means that there exist two positive constants
C_{1},C_{2}
, independent of
u
such that
C_{1}\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;H^{l-p})}\leq\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})}\leq C_{2}\|u\|_{\cap_{p=0}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;H^{l-p})}
.

Combining this with (
4.25
), (
4.26
), (
4.29
), and (
4.30
), we conclude that for each
k\leq l
,

\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{K}\big[G(\gamma)u\big]\|_{C(0,T;L^{2})}\leq C(n,M,C_{\Omega},T,\nu_{1},l)\|u\|_{\cap_{p=1}^{l}C^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{l-p})}.

(4.32)

Thus, the proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

Combining the fact that the coefficients have the expanding form seen in Remark
4.2
, by Lemma
4.3
, we first obtain that

\begin{split}\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}-\delta_{ij})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}&\leq C_{ab}\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|v^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}},~i,j=1,\cdots,n,\\
\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}b_{k}^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}&\leq C_{ab}\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|v^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}},~k=1,\cdots,n,\\
\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}(b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-1)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}&\leq C_{ab}\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|v^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}},\\
\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}(\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}&\leq C_{ab}\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|v^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}},\end{split}

(4.33)

where
C_{ab}
is a constant depending on
\nu_{1},s,T,\Omega
and the bounds of
a_{ij,k},i,j=1,\cdots,n,k=0,1,\cdots,n+1
,
b_{i,k},i,k=0,1,\cdots,n+1
and
\tilde{b}_{i},i=0,1,\cdots,n+1
, independent of
\alpha
and
v^{(\alpha-1)}
.

We can then consider the estimations on
F^{(\beta+1)}
and
H^{(\beta+1)}
. Based on the expressions (
4.11
) and (
4.12
), we need to estimate the following terms:

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-b_{0}^{(\alpha-1)})\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}},\qquad\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}-\tilde{b}^{(\alpha-1)})\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}},

(4.34)

and

\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(b_{i}^{(\alpha)}-b_{i}^{(\alpha-1)})\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}},\qquad\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}-a_{ij}^{(\alpha-1)})\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}},

for any non-negative integer
p\leq s-2
.

We only deal with
\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-b_{0}^{(\alpha-1)})\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}
, other terms are the same.
Note the fact that

\displaystyle b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-b_{0}^{(\alpha-1)}

\displaystyle=b_{0}(t,x,v^{(\alpha-1)},v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla v^{(\alpha-1)})-b_{0}(t,x,v^{(\alpha-2)},v_{t}^{(\alpha-2)},\nabla v^{(\alpha-2)})

\displaystyle=b_{0,v}(v^{(\alpha-1)}-v^{(\alpha-2)})+b_{0,v_{t}}(v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}-v_{t}^{(\alpha-2)})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{0,vi}(v_{x_{i}}^{(\alpha-1)}-v_{x_{i}}^{(\alpha-2)})

\displaystyle=b_{0,v}V^{(\alpha-1)}+b_{0,v_{t}}V_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{0,vi}V_{x_{i}}^{(\alpha-1)}

where

\begin{cases}b_{0,v}=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial v}b_{0}(t,x,\theta v^{(\alpha-1)},v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla v^{(\alpha-1)}){\mathrm{d}}\theta,\\
b_{0,v_{t}}=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{t}}b_{0}(t,x,v^{(\alpha-1)},\theta v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla v^{(\alpha-1)}){\mathrm{d}}\theta,\\
b_{0,vi}=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{x_{i}}}b_{0}(t,x,v^{(\alpha-1)},v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)},\theta v_{x_{i}}^{(\alpha-1)}){\mathrm{d}}\theta.\end{cases}

Since we have assume that

\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}\leq\nu_{1},\quad\sum_{i=1,2}\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|\partial_{t}^{p}v^{(\alpha-i)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}}\leq\nu_{1},

(4.35)

for some small
\nu_{1}
.
Then, noting that
s-1\geq n+1
, we can apply Lemma
4.3
with
G=b_{0,v}
(resp.
b_{0,v_{t}},b_{0,vi}
) and
l=s-1
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{p=0}^{s-2}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}(b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-b_{0}^{(\alpha-1)})\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-2-p}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{ab}\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}(v^{(\alpha-1)}-v^{(\alpha-2)})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{ab}\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}},

where
C^{\prime}_{ab}
is a constant independent with
\alpha
and
V^{(\alpha-1)}
.

Thus applying Lemma
4.3
again with
G=a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}-a_{ij}^{(\alpha-1)}
and
l=s-1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{p=0}^{s-2}\big\|\partial_{t}^{p}((b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-b_{0}^{(\alpha-1)})v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)})\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-2-p}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{ab}(\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}})(\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}})

(4.37)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{ab}(\sum_{p=0}^{s-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1-p}})(\sum_{p=0}^{s}\|\partial_{t}^{p}v^{(\alpha-1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-p}}),

where
C^{\prime\prime}_{ab}
is a positive constant independent of
\alpha
.
Similarly, we can obtain the estimations of other terms in
F^{(\beta+1)}
and
H^{(\beta+1)}
. Thus we complete the proof.
∎

We are now in a position to commence the proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

To obtain the spatial norm estimates of the system solutions (
4.14
) and (
4.15
), we need to establish the following norm estimates:
There exist constants
\varepsilon_{lem},M,\delta
,
{C}_{V,mid,l},{C}_{Z,mid,l},{C}_{v,mid,l},{C}_{z,mid,l}
for any
l=0,\cdots,s-2
, independent of
\varepsilon
and
\alpha
, such that for any
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{lem}
and any time
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{m+1}V^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-m}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{m}V^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l+1-m}}^{2}\leq(1-\delta)^{2\alpha-2}{C}_{V,mid,l}^{2}M^{2l}\varepsilon^{2},

(4.38)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{m+1}Z^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-m}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{m}Z^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l+l-m}}^{2}\leq(1-\delta)^{2\alpha-2}{C}_{Z,mid,l}^{2}M^{2l}\varepsilon^{2},

and

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{m+1}v^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l+1-m}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{m}v^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l+2-m}}^{2}\leq{C}_{v,mid,l}^{2}M^{2l+2}\varepsilon^{2},

(4.39)

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{m+1}z^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l+1-m}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{m}z^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l+2-m}}^{2}\leq{C}_{z,mid,l}^{2}M^{2l+2}\varepsilon^{2},

for any
m=0,\cdots,l
.

Taking
l=s-2
and
m=0,1
directly, we can immediately derive (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) from (
4.38
) and (
4.39
). Therefore, we will focus on proving (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) in the following.

We prove (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) by deduction.
The proof of the assertions (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) will be systematically approached through a sequence of methodical steps, delineated as follows:

1.

Establish the base case by demonstrating that (
4.38
), and (
4.39
) hold true when
\alpha=1
;

2.

Establish the base case by demonstrating that (
4.38
) holds true when
l=0
;

3.

Proceed to the inductive step for (
4.38
) and (
4.39
), where it is to be shown that for
\alpha=\beta+1\geq 2,s-2\geq l\geq k+1\geq 2
, the assertion (
4.38
) is valid. Similarly, for
\alpha=\beta+1\geq 2,s-3\geq l\geq k+1\geq 1
, the assertion (
4.39
) is valid, given that the conditions (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) are presupposed to be valid for
\alpha\leq\beta,l\leq k\geq 1
;

4.

Proceed to the inductive step for (
4.39
), where it is to be shown that for
\alpha=\beta+1\geq 2
, the assertion (
4.39
) is valid, given that the conditions (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) are presupposed to be valid for
\alpha=\beta
.

4.2.1
Basic step 1: The case of
\alpha=1
.

We first note that
V^{(1)}=v^{(1)},Z^{(1)}=z^{(1)}
, which satisfy the following equations:

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\Delta+\partial_{t})V^{(1)}=-2\chi Z_{t}^{(1)},(\partial_{t}^{2}-\Delta-\partial_{t})Z^{(1)}=0.

(4.40)

Additionally, we have the initial and terminal conditions:

(V^{(1)}(0),V_{t}^{(1)}(0))=(v^{(1)}(0),v_{t}^{(1)}(0)),(Z^{(1)}(T),Z_{t}^{(1)}(T))=(0,0).

(4.41)

By the well-posedness theory of linear wave equations, there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
, such that for any
t\in[0,T]
, for any
k=0,\cdots,s-1,

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-k}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k+1}Z^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-k-1}}^{2}\leq e^{c_{0}(t-T)}\big(\|\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(1)}(T)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-k}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k+1}Z^{(1)}(T)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-k-1}}^{2}\big)=0.\end{split}

This implies that
-2\chi Z_{t}^{(1)}\equiv 0
. Consequently, using the equation for
V
in (
4.40
), we obtain that for any
l\leq s
and
k=0,\cdots,l
,

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k+1}V^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k-1}}^{2}&\leq e^{-c_{0}t}\big(\|\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(1)}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k+1}V^{(1)}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k-1}}^{2}\big).\end{split}

Using the relation (
4.41
), we have

\Delta^{m}V^{(1)}(0)=\Delta^{m}v^{(1)}(0),\quad\Delta^{m}\partial_{t}V^{(1)}(0)=\Delta^{m}\partial_{t}v^{(1)}(0),

for any integer
m\geq 0
. Applying the operator
\partial_{t}^{k-2}
the equation for
V^{(1)}
in (
4.40
) with
Z\equiv 0
, we obtain that for any
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(1)}+\partial_{t}^{k-1}V^{(1)}=\Delta(\partial_{t}^{k-2}V^{(1)}).

(4.42)

Using this recursive relation, we can express

\partial^{k}_{t}V^{(1)}(0)=\partial_{t}^{m}\Delta^{\frac{k-m}{2}}V^{(1)}(0)+\sum_{p=0}^{\frac{k-m}{2}}\Delta^{p}\big(C_{k,p,1}V^{(1)}(0)+C_{k,p,2}\partial_{t}V^{(1)}(0)\big).

where
m=\frac{1-(-1)^{k}}{2}
,
C_{k,p,1}
and
C_{k,p,2}
are constants depending only on
k,p
.
By elliptic regularity theory, for any
u\in\mathcal{H}^{s}
and
s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s
, there exists a constant
C_{s_{1},s_{2}}
depending only on
s_{1},s_{2}
and
\Omega
, such that
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s_{1}}}\leq C_{s_{1},s_{2}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s_{2}}}
.

Thus, for any
l=1,\cdots,s-1
and
k=0,\cdots,l
, we have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(1)}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k+1}V^{(1)}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-k-1}}^{2}&\leq C_{l}(\|v^{(1)}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l}}^{2}+\|v^{(1)}_{t}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{l-1}}^{2})\\
&\leq C_{Vini,l}(\|y^{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}^{2}+\|y^{1}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s-1}}^{2}),\end{split}

for some constant
C_{l},C_{Vini,l}>0
depending only on
k
,
s
and
\Omega
.
Together with the smallness assumption on the initial data
(y^{0},y^{1})
, we conclude that if the constants
C_{V,mid,l},C_{v,mid,l}
are setting by

C_{V,mid,l}=2C_{Vini,l},\quad C_{v,mid,l}=\frac{2(1-\delta)^{2}}{1-(1-\delta)^{2}}C_{Vini,l},

(4.43)

and
C_{Z,mid,l},C_{z,mid,l}
are setting by

C_{Z,mid,l}=\frac{1}{4T}C_{V,mid,l},\quad C_{z,mid,l}=\frac{1}{4T}C_{v,mid,l},

(4.44)

then the estimates (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) hold for the case
\alpha=1
.

4.2.2
Basic step 2: To prove (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) when
l=0
for any
\alpha\geq 2

When
l=0
, we need to establish estimates for the coefficients. Since (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) are assumed to hold for
\alpha=\beta\geq 1
, we can choose

\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\varepsilon_{nu}
sufficiently small such that

\max_{0\leq k\leq s}C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\varepsilon_{nu}\leq 1=:\nu_{1}.

(4.45)

Here
C_{v,mid,k},k=0,1,\cdots,s
are defined by (
4.43
) and
M
would be choosing later.

By Lemma
4.3
and (
4.33
) in Remark
4.2
with
\nu_{1}=1
, for any
t\in[0,T]
, we obtain for each
\alpha\leq\beta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|b^{(\alpha)}_{0}-1\big\|_{\cap_{k=0}^{s-1}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1-k})}+\big\|a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}-\delta_{ij}\big\|_{\cap_{k=0}^{s-1}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1-k})}\\
&+\big\|\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{\cap_{k=0}^{s-1}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1-k})}+\big\|b^{(\alpha)}_{i}\big\|_{\cap_{k=0}^{s-1}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1-k})}\leq 4C_{\Omega}\max_{k}\{C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\}\varepsilon,\end{split}

(4.46)

where
C_{\Omega}
is a constant depending on the expression of coefficients in Remark
4.2
, independent of
\alpha
and
\varepsilon
.

Next, we choose
\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\min\{\varepsilon_{Hs},\varepsilon_{nu}\}
with

4C_{\Omega}\max_{k}\{C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\}\varepsilon_{Hs}=\varepsilon_{\mathcal{H}^{s}},

(4.47)

where
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}
is defined by (
2.69
) with
l=s\geq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor+2
.
Thus, the coefficients meet the conditions (
2.69
) with
l=s\geq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor+2
. Applying Corollary (
2.7
), we conclude that System (
4.4
)-(
4.3
) admits a unique solution
(v^{(\alpha)},z^{(\alpha)})\in\cap_{k=0}^{1}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-k})\times\cap_{k=0}^{1}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-k})
. Consequently, we have
V^{(\alpha)},Z^{(\alpha)}\in\cap_{k=0}^{1}C^{k}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-k})
.

We can use well-posedness of the system of
Z^{(\alpha)}
to transform the estimate (
4.38
), which holds for any time
t\in[0,T]
, into the following estimates at the terminal time
T
.

Claim 4.4
.

For any
\alpha\geq 1
,
Z^{(\alpha)}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}Z^{(\alpha)}(T)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|Z^{(\alpha)}(T)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq C_{Z_{T},mid,0}(1-\delta)^{2\alpha-2}\varepsilon^{2},

(4.48)

for some constant
C_{Z_{T},mid,0}
independent of
\alpha
and
\varepsilon
.

Proof..

Since
Z^{(1)}\equiv 0
for any
t\in[0,T]
, the estimate (
4.48
) holds trivially for
\alpha=1
. We now proceed by induction. Assume that (
4.48
) holds for all
\alpha\leq\beta
.

Referring to the proof of the linear system, for any
\beta
, we define

w^{(\beta)}=v^{(\beta)}+z^{(\beta)},\qquad W^{(\beta)}=w^{(\beta)}-w^{(\beta-1)}.

From the initial data of the
z
-system (
4.3
), we derive

W^{(\beta)}_{t}(T)=Z^{(\beta+1)}(T),\quad W^{(\beta)}_{t}(T)=Z^{(\beta+1)}_{t}(T).

(4.49)

For convenience, we define the energy functional: for any
U\in C(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})\cap C^{1}(0,T;L^{2})
,

E_{\alpha}(U)(t)=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\int_{\Omega}|U_{t}(t)|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}U_{x_{i}}(t)U_{x_{j}}(t){\mathrm{d}}x\bigg).

(4.50)

Based on the coefficient estimates (
4.46
), and using embedding theory, we derive the
C^{1}
estimates for the coefficients:

\begin{cases}\|a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}-\delta_{ij}\|_{C^{1}((0,T)\times\Omega)}\leq C_{coe,C^{1}}C_{\Omega}\max_{k}\{C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\}\varepsilon,\quad i,j=1,\cdots,n,\\
\|b_{0}^{(\alpha)}-1\|_{C^{1}((0,T)\times\Omega)}\leq C_{coe,C^{1}}C_{\Omega}\max_{k}\{C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\}\varepsilon,\\
\|\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}\|_{C^{1}((0,T)\times\Omega)}\leq C_{coe,C^{1}}C_{\Omega}\max_{k}\{C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\}\varepsilon,\\
\|b_{k}^{(\alpha)}\|_{C^{1}((0,T)\times\Omega)}\leq C_{coe,C^{1}}C_{\Omega}\max_{k}\{C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\}\varepsilon,\quad k=1,\cdots,n,\end{cases}

(4.51)

where
C_{coe,C^{1}}
depends only on
\Omega
and
n
.
Then we can show that for any
\alpha\leq\beta+1
, for any
U\in C(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})\cap C^{1}(0,T;L^{2})
,

(2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon)E_{\alpha}(U)\leq\|\partial_{t}U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|U\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq(2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon)E_{\alpha}(U),

(4.52)

where
C_{coe,1}=nC_{coe,C^{1}}C_{\Omega}\max_{k}\{C_{v,mid,k}M^{k+1}\}
is independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

We now consider the following expression:

\begin{split}E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta+1)})-E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})&=E_{\beta}(W^{(\beta)})-E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})\\
&=E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})+\int_{\Omega}\big(V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot Z^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}\big){\mathrm{d}}x.\end{split}

(4.53)

We first estimate
E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})
. Multiplying the equation for
V^{(\beta)}
by
V_{t}^{(\beta)}
,
we derive the following inequality:

\displaystyle V^{(\beta)}_{tt}V_{t}^{(\beta)}+b_{0}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{t}V_{t}^{(\beta)}

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}V_{t}^{(\beta)}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}V_{t}^{(\beta)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}V_{t}^{(\beta)}

(4.54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F^{(\beta)}V_{t}^{(\beta)}-2(\chi Z_{t}^{(\beta)})V_{t}^{(\beta)}.

By Stokes’ formula, we have

\int_{\Omega}V^{(\beta)}_{tt}V_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x=\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\Omega}\big|V_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.55)

Utilizing the symmetry property
a^{(\beta+1)}_{ij}=a_{ji}^{(\beta+1)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x-\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a^{(\beta)}_{ijt}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.56)

In view of (
4.51
), by the inequality
ab\leq\frac{1}{2}(a^{2}+b^{2})
and Poincaré’s inequality, we obtain that there exists a constant
C_{coe}
independent of
\varepsilon
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}(b^{(\beta)}_{0}-1)|V_{t}^{(\beta)}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\right|+\left|\int_{\Omega}\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}V_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}V_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x\right|+\left|\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a^{(\beta)}_{ijt}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C_{coe}\varepsilon E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)}).

(4.57)

To estimate
F^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{t}
, we recall
(
4.24
) in Lemma
4.1
. Combining this with the induction hypothesis that (
4.15
) holds for
\alpha=\beta\geq 2
, we obtain that for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|F^{(\beta)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\tilde{C}_{F}(1-\delta)^{\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

where
\tilde{C}_{F}
is a constant independent with
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.
Applying Hölder’s inequality, we then have for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{\Omega}|F^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{t}|{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3},

(4.58)

where
\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F}
is a constant independent with
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

Next, we observe that

\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}\big|V_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x+2\int_{\Omega}V_{t}^{(\beta)}(\chi Z_{t}^{(\beta)}){\mathrm{d}}x\geq-\int_{\Omega}\big|\chi\cdot Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x,\end{split}

(4.59)

Combining (
4.55
)–(
4.58
) and (
4.59
), we arrive at

\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})\leq\big\|\chi\cdot Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{coe}\varepsilon E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})+\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}.

Applying Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain that for all

t\in[0,T]
,

E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})\leq C_{VtoZ}(\varepsilon)\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big|\chi\cdot Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+T\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}\right),

(4.60)

where
C_{VtoZ}(\varepsilon)=e^{TC_{coe}\varepsilon}
is a constant that is independent of
\delta
and
\beta
.

We next estimate

\int_{\Omega}V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot Z^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x.

Multiplying (
4.9
) by
Z_{t}^{(\beta)}
, (
4.10
) by
V_{t}^{(\beta)}
, and integrating over
\Omega
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\Big(Z_{t}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}

(4.61)

\displaystyle+V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}V_{t}^{(\beta)}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle+2\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}H^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x.

By integration by parts, we derive

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\Big(Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+V_{t}^{(\beta)}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x

(4.62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}Z_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\partial_{t}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}\big)Z_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x,

and

\int_{\Omega}\Big(Z_{t}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{tt}+V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}_{tt}\big){\mathrm{d}}x=\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\Omega}V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.63)

Using the coefficient estimates (
4.51
), the inequality
ab\leq\frac{1}{2}(a^{2}+b^{2})
and Poincaré’s inequality, we obtain:

\begin{split}\left|\int_{\Omega}\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x\right|\leq C_{1}\varepsilon(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})),\\
\left|\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}{\mathrm{d}}x\right|\leq C_{1}\varepsilon(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})),\\
\left|\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\partial_{t}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}Z_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x\right|\leq C_{1}\varepsilon(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})).\end{split}

(4.64)

Similar to (
4.58
), we obtain that for any
t\in[0,T]

\|H^{(\beta)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\tilde{C}_{H}(1-\delta)^{\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2},\int_{\Omega}|H^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}_{t}|\leq\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{3},

(4.65)

where
\tilde{C}_{H},\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}
are constants independent with
\beta,\delta
and
\varepsilon
.

Combining (
4.61
)–(
4.64
) with (
4.65
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\Omega}\Big(Z_{t}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{t}+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}Z_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x+2\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3C_{1}\varepsilon(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)}))+\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{3}.

Integrating with respect to

t
over the interval
(0,T)
and using the initial condition
(V^{(\beta)}(0),V_{t}^{(\beta)}(0))=(0,0)
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\Big(Z_{t}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{t}+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}Z_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x\Big|_{t=T}+2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(3C_{1}\varepsilon(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)}))+\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{3}\right){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3C_{1}\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})){\mathrm{d}}t+T\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{3}.

Combining this with the energy estimate (
4.60
) of
V^{(\beta)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle E_{\beta}\big(V^{(\beta)}(T)\big)+\int_{\Omega}V_{t}^{(\beta)}Z_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot Z^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x

(4.66)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3C_{1}\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)})){\mathrm{d}}t+T(C_{VtoZ}(\varepsilon)\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F}+\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H})(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}

\displaystyle+C_{VtoZ}(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big|\chi\cdot Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Since (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) are assumed to hold for
\alpha=\beta
, we have

(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)})+E_{\beta}(V^{(\beta)}))\leq C_{2}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

(4.67)

where
C_{2}:=C^{2}_{Z,mid,0}+C^{2}_{V,mid,0}
.

Taking
\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\varepsilon_{2,0}
, where
\varepsilon_{2,0}
is sufficiently small, such that
C_{VtoZ}(\varepsilon_{2,0})=e^{TC_{coe}\varepsilon_{2,0}}\leq\frac{3}{2}
, and noting that
0\leq\chi_{\omega}(x)\leq 1
, we obtain

C_{VtoZ}(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big|\chi\cdot Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(4.68)

Combining (
4.66
), (
4.68
) with (
4.53
), we deduce that

E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta+1)}(T))\leq E_{\beta}\big(Z^{(\beta)}(T)\big)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C_{3}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{3},

(4.69)

where
C_{3}=3TC_{1}C_{2}+\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F}+\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}
is a constant independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

Choosing
\varepsilon_{lem}
small enough, such that

C(C_{\Omega},\nu_{0},n,s)\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\varepsilon_{obs},

(4.70)

where
\varepsilon_{obs}
is given in Theorem
2.8
, and recalling (
4.65
), we can then apply Theorem
2.8
to System (
4.10
) for
Z^{(\beta)}
, and obtain the following observability inequality

E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)}(T))\leq D\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\big|Z_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C_{Z,0}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{4},

(4.71)

for some constant
D
and
C_{Z,0}
. Thus we obtain

\displaystyle E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)))\leq

\displaystyle\Big(1-\frac{1}{2D}\Big)E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta)}(T))+C_{Z,0}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{4}+C_{3}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{3}.

(4.72)

Taking
\delta>0
small enough such that

1-\frac{1}{2D}\leq(1-\delta)^{3},

(4.73)

and recalling (
4.52
), we obtain that

\displaystyle~\big\|Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+\big\|Z_{t}^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~(1-\delta)^{3}\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}C_{Z_{T},mid,0}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+(2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon)^{-1}C_{Z,0}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{4}+(2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon)^{-1}C_{3}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-4}\varepsilon^{3}.

Taking
\varepsilon_{3}
small enough such that

(1-\delta)\cdot\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon_{3}}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon_{3}}\leq 1-\frac{\delta}{2},

(4.74)

and

\left(1-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\left(C_{Z_{T},mid,0}+(2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon_{3})^{-1}(1-\delta)^{-4}(C_{Z,0}\varepsilon_{3}+C_{3})\varepsilon_{3}\right)<C_{Z_{T},mid,0}.

(4.75)

Therefore, choosing
\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\varepsilon_{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+\big\|Z_{t}^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{Z_{T},mid,0}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{2}.

(4.76)

Thus, we complete the proof of Claim
4.4
.
∎

Similarly, using the standard energy estimate for
Z^{(\beta+1)}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta+1)})(t)\leq C_{ZtoZ_{T}}(\varepsilon)\left(E_{\beta}(Z^{(\beta+1)})(T)+T\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{3}\right),

(4.77)

where
C_{ZtoZ_{T}}(\varepsilon)=e^{C_{Z_{T}}T\varepsilon}
and
C_{Z_{T}},\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}
are constants independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

Choosing
\varepsilon_{lem}<\varepsilon_{ZtoZT}
small enough and
C_{Z,mid,0}=2C_{Z_{T},mid,0}
such that

C_{ZtoZ_{T}}(\varepsilon_{ZtoZT})\leq\frac{3}{2},

(4.78)

and

\frac{3}{2}\cdot\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon_{ZtoZT}}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon_{ZtoZT}}C_{Z_{T},mid,0}+T\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{H}(1-\delta)^{-2}\varepsilon_{ZtoZT}\leq C_{Z,mid,0},

(4.79)

we ensure that
Z^{(\beta+1)}
in (
4.38
) holds for
l=0
.

Finally, given that
v^{(1)}=V^{(1)}
and relationships between
v^{(\alpha)}
and
V^{(\alpha)}
, we can derive the following inequality by (
4.38
):

\big\|\partial_{t}v^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|v^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}=\big\|\sum_{\beta=1}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\sum_{\beta=1}^{\alpha}V^{(\beta)}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq\frac{(1-\delta)^{2}-(1-\delta)^{2\alpha}}{1-(1-\delta)^{2}}C^{2}_{V,mid,1}\varepsilon^{2}.

Observing that
\frac{(1-\delta)^{2}-(1-\delta)^{2\alpha}}{1-(1-\delta)^{2}}\leq\frac{(1-\delta)^{2}}{1-(1-\delta)^{2}}
and invoking the setting in (
4.43
), we can thereby conclude that the inequality (
4.39
) holds for the case
l=0
.

4.2.3
Inductive step 1: To prove (
4.38
) for
l\geq 1
and all
\alpha\geq 2

We first consider the equations for
\partial_{t}^{l}V^{(\beta+1)}
and
\partial_{t}^{l}Z^{(\beta+1)}
for
l\geq 1
.
To achieve this, we apply the differential operator
\partial_{t}^{k-2}

to Equations (
4.9
) and (
4.3
), resulting in the following equations:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{tt}+b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}

(4.80)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}=-2\chi\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}Z_{t}^{(\beta+1)}+\partial_{t}^{s-1}F^{(\beta+1)}+F^{(\beta+1,k)},

and

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{tt}-b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}_{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}

(4.81)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}=\partial_{t}^{k}H^{(\beta+1)}+H^{(\beta+1,k)},

where

\displaystyle F^{(\beta+1,k)}=

\displaystyle~b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}^{k}\Big(b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\Big),

and

\displaystyle H^{(\beta+1,k)}=

\displaystyle b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}^{k}\Big(b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\beta+1)}Z^{(\beta+1)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta+1)}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{i}\Big).

Combining (
4.24
) in Lemma
4.1
with the assumption that (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) hold for
\alpha\leq\beta
, we obtain that for any
t\in[0,T]
,
k=1,\cdots,s-2

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}^{k}F^{(\beta)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\tilde{C}_{F,k}(1-\delta)^{\beta-1}\varepsilon^{2},\|\partial_{t}^{k}H^{(\beta)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\tilde{C}_{H,k}(1-\delta)^{\beta-1}\varepsilon^{2},\end{split}

where
\tilde{C}_{F,k}
and
\tilde{C}_{H,k}
are constants independent of
M
,
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

Next, we state the following claim:

Claim 4.5
.

For any
\beta\geq 1
and
1\leq k\leq s-2
,
F^{(\beta+1,k)},H^{(\beta+1,k)}\in C(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})
and
there exist constants
C_{F,k},C_{H,k}
, independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
, such that

\begin{split}\|F^{(\beta+1,k)}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C_{F,k}\varepsilon\big(\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{\beta+1}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}^{p+1}V^{\beta+1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}^{p}Z^{(\beta+1)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big),\\
\|H^{(\beta+1,k)}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C_{H,k}\varepsilon\big(\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}Z^{\beta+1}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}^{p+1}Z^{\beta+1}\|_{L^{2}}\big).\end{split}

(4.82)

Proof..

We first estimate
F^{(\beta+1,k)}
; the estimate for
H^{(\beta+1,k)}
follows similarly. By expanding

F^{(\beta+1,k)}
into commutators, we focus on the first term:

b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t})=-\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}C_{k}^{l}\partial_{t}^{l}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}\partial_{t}^{k-l}(b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}).

(4.83)

Each term in the above expression contains at least the first-order time derivative of
(b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}
, and at most the
k-1
-th time derivative of
V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}
and
k
-th time derivative of
(b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}
, Consequently, by Hölder’s inequality, we have:

\|\big[b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}-\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}))\big]\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(s,l)(\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\|_{L^{2})})(\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{\beta+1}\|_{L^{2}})

(4.84)

By (
4.46
), we have
\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\|_{L^{2})}\leq C\varepsilon
for some constant
C
.

Next, we consider the term

\partial_{t}^{k}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}.

When
k\leq s-2
, similar estimates can be derived for the remaining commutators in
F^{(\beta+1,k)}
, except for the term:

\|\big(\partial_{t}^{k}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}\big)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon(\sum_{p=0}^{k-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\beta+1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}).

We observe that

\sum_{p=0}^{k-1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\beta+1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}\leq C(\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\beta+1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+\sum_{p=0}^{k+1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\beta+1)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}Z^{(\beta+1)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

(4.85)

Indeed, using Equation (
4.10
) and the conditions (
4.51
) on the coefficients, we deduce that for any
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\big\|\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k-2}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-2}V^{(\beta+1)}_{tt}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|b_{0}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k-2}V^{(\beta+1)}_{t}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{b}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k-2}V^{(\beta+1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k-2}V^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-2}F^{(\beta+1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|F^{(\beta+1,k-2)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\big\|2\chi\partial_{t}^{k-2}Z_{t}^{(\beta+1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By the expression of
F^{(\beta+1,k)}
and the fact that
F^{(\beta+1,0)}=0
, combined with the conditions (
4.51
), we can deduce that

\big\|\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}\partial_{t}^{k-2}Z^{(\beta+1)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(\sum_{p=0}^{k}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\beta+1)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+\sum_{p=0}^{k+1}\|\partial_{t}^{p}V^{(\beta+1)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big).

Since
\partial_{t}^{k-2}Z^{(\beta+1)}
is well-defined and belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{2}
for each
k=2,\cdots,s
and due to the conditions (
4.51
) of
a_{ij}^{(\beta+1)}
, elliptic theory implies the desired estimate.

Thus, the proof of the claim is complete.
∎

Thanks to the estimations of
(F^{(\beta+1,k)},H^{(\beta+1,k)})
in Claim
4.5
, together with the coefficient estimates (
4.51
), we can apply Corollary
2.7
to obtain the well-posedness of
\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)}
and
\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}
in
C(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-k})
.

To complete the proof of (
4.38
), by induction, it suffices to prove (
4.38
) for the case
l=k,\alpha=\beta+1
under the assumption that (
4.38
) holds for
l\leq k-1,k\geq 1
and for any
\alpha
, as well as for
l\leq s-1,\alpha\leq\beta
.

Similar to the case
l=0
, we now prove the following inequality:

Claim 4.6
.

There exists a small positive constant
\varepsilon_{ZT}
, such that if
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{ZT}
, then for any integer
\alpha\geq 1
, any positive integer
l\leq s-2
,
Z^{(\alpha)}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{l+1}Z^{(\alpha)}(T)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{l}Z^{(\alpha)}(T)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq C_{Z_{T},mid,l}(1-\delta)^{2\alpha-2}{M^{2l}}\varepsilon^{2},

(4.86)

where
C_{Z_{T},mid,l}
and
M
are positive constants independent of
\alpha
and
\varepsilon
.

Proof..

By employing mathematical induction, we assume that (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) hold for all
\alpha\leq\beta
when
l\leq s-1
, as well as for all
\alpha
when
l\leq k-1
and
s-1\geq k\geq 1
. Under these assumptions, it remains to prove that (
4.86
) holds for
\alpha=\beta+1
and
l=k
.
For notational simplicity, we define
\tilde{V}^{(\beta+1,k)}=\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta+1)},\tilde{Z}^{(\beta+1,k)}=\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}
and for any
t\in[0,T]
, we define

\tilde{W}^{(\beta+1,k)}=\tilde{V}^{(\beta+1,k)}+\tilde{Z}^{(\beta+1,k)},\tilde{w}^{(\beta+1,k)}=\tilde{v}^{(\beta+1,k)}+\tilde{z}^{(\beta+1,k)}.

The estimates (
4.48
) for
l=k
directly come from the estimation of
E_{\beta}(\tilde{W}^{(\beta,k)}(T))-E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta,k)}(T))
and the relation between
E_{\beta}(\tilde{W}^{(\beta,k)}(T))
and
E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta+1,k)}(T))
.

The estimate of
E_{\beta}(\tilde{W}^{(\beta,k)}(T))-E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta,k)}(T))
follows a similar approach to the case
l=0
. We first observe that:

\displaystyle E_{\beta}(\tilde{W}^{(\beta,k)}(T))-E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta,k)}(T))

(4.87)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\int_{\Omega}\tilde{V}_{t}^{(\beta)}\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x\bigg)\Big|_{t=T}+E_{\beta}\big(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}(T)\big).

Similarly, by multiplying the equation for

\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}
by
\partial_{t}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}
and integrating over
\Omega
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}E_{\beta}(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)})\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\chi\cdot\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{V,2,k}\varepsilon E_{\beta}(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)})

\displaystyle+\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}+\bigg|\int_{\Omega}F^{(\beta,k)}\partial_{t}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x\bigg|,

for some constants
C_{V,2,k},\tilde{\tilde{C}}_{F,k}
, independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

Since (
4.38
) is assumed to hold for
\alpha=\beta
, by Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
4.5
, we have:

\bigg|\int_{\Omega}F^{(\beta,k)}\partial_{t}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x\bigg|\leq C_{V,4,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{3}.

for some constant
C_{V,4,k}
, independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
. Thus, by applying Gronwall’s inequality, we immediately obtain:

E_{\beta}(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}(t))\leq C_{\tilde{V}to\tilde{V}}(\varepsilon)\left(E_{\beta}(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}(0))+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big|\chi\cdot\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C_{V,5,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}\right),

(4.88)

where
C_{\tilde{V}to\tilde{V}}(\varepsilon)\leq C_{\tilde{V}to\tilde{V}}(\varepsilon_{2,k})=\frac{3}{2}<2
is a constant when choosing
\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\varepsilon_{2,k}
.

The next step is to estimate
E_{\beta}(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}(0))
. The equation (
4.9
) of
V^{(\beta)}
can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}V^{(\beta)}+\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}=\Delta V^{(\beta)}-2\chi\cdot\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta)}+V_{error}^{(\beta)},

(4.89)

where

\displaystyle V_{error}^{(\beta)}:=

\displaystyle~\big(1-b_{0}^{(\beta)}\big)V_{t}^{(\beta)}-\big(\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}\big)V^{(\beta)}-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}V_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}

(4.90)

\displaystyle+F^{(\beta)}+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\Big(\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}-\delta_{ij}\big)V_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}\Big)_{x_{j}}.

Since (
4.38
) are assumed to hold for
\alpha=\beta
and recalling the estimates on coefficients, applying Lemma
4.3
with
G=V_{error}^{(\beta)}
, we have for any
m\leq s-2-k

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}V_{error}^{(\beta)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}\leq C_{V,error}\big((1-\delta)^{\beta-1}M^{k+m+2}\varepsilon^{2}\big),

(4.91)

for some constant
C_{V,error}
independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.
Differentiating (
4.89
) with respect to
t
for
k-2
times, we obtain:

When
k\geq 1
is odd,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta)}=\partial_{t}\Delta^{\frac{k-1}{2}}V^{(\beta)}-\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{k-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2\chi Z^{(\beta)}_{t}+\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}-V_{error}^{(\beta)}\Big),

(4.92)

when
k\geq 2
is even,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta)}=\Delta^{\frac{k}{2}}V^{(\beta)}-\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\partial_{t}^{k-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2\chi Z^{(\beta)}_{t}+\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}-V_{error}^{(\beta)}\Big).

(4.93)

We note that
\chi
is a smooth bounded function so that there exist a sequence constants
C_{\chi,p},p=0,1,\cdots,k
such that for any
u\in\mathcal{H}^{k}
,

\sum_{p=0}^{l}\|\Delta^{p}(2\chi\partial_{t}^{m}u)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\chi,l}\sum_{p=0}^{l}\|\Delta^{p}\partial_{t}^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}=C_{\chi,l}\sum_{p=0}^{l}\|\partial_{t}^{m}u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2p}}

Noting that from initial data for
V^{(\beta)}
in (
4.9
), we have
(\Delta^{m}V^{(\beta)}(0),\partial_{t}\Delta^{m}V^{(\beta)}(0))=(0,0)
for any integer
m\geq 0
. Moreover, since (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) hold for
\alpha=\beta
, we obtain

\displaystyle~\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}V^{(\beta)}(0)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}V^{(\beta)}(0)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}

(4.94)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}C^{2}_{V,mid,i}M^{2i}+C_{\chi,k}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i}M^{2i}\bigg)(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}+kC^{2}_{V,error}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}M^{2k}\varepsilon^{4},

Similarly, by multiplying the equation for
\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}
by
\partial_{t}\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}
and adding it to the equation for
\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}
multiplied by
\partial_{t}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}
, and integrating over
\Omega
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\tilde{V}_{t}^{(\beta)}\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big|_{t=T}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C_{5,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}+\int_{\Omega}\tilde{V}_{t}^{(\beta)}\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big|_{t=0}.

If we regard
\partial_{t}^{k}F^{\beta}+F^{(\beta,k)}
as an external force term
f
, then the equations satisfied by
(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)},\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)})
have the same coefficients as those for
(V^{(\beta)},Z^{(\beta)})
. Therefore, we can then apply Theorem
2.8
to equation for
\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}
, to obtain the following observability inequality:

E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}(T))\leq D\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\big|\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}_{t}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C_{6}\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{k}F^{\beta}+F^{(\beta,k)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.95)

for the same constant
D
and some constant
C_{6}>0
independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

Recalling (
4.24
) in Lemma
4.1
and (
4.82
) in Lemma (
4.5
), we have

\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{k}F^{\beta}+F^{(\beta,k)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C_{F,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{4}.

Next, we estimate the term
\int_{\Omega}\tilde{V}_{t}^{(\beta)}\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x\big|_{t=0}
. By Hölder’s inequality, this term is bounded by

4E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)})^{\frac{1}{2}}E_{\beta}(\tilde{V}^{(\beta)})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|_{t=0}.

Using (
4.38
) with
\alpha=\beta
and (
4.94
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\tilde{V}_{t}^{(\beta)}\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{(\beta)}\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}_{x_{i}}\cdot\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)}_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x\big|_{t=T}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\chi\big|\tilde{Z}_{t}^{(\beta)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\tilde{C}_{5,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}

\displaystyle+C_{Z,mid,k}M^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{V,mid,i}M^{2i}+\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}C_{\chi,i}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i}M^{2i}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2},

where
\tilde{C}_{5,k}=C_{5,k}+C_{Z,mid,k}C_{V,error}^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\delta)^{2}
. Thus combining this, (
4.94
) with (
4.73
), we obtain

\displaystyle E_{\beta}(\tilde{W}^{(\beta)})(T)\leq~

\displaystyle(1-\delta)^{3}E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta)})(T)

\displaystyle+C_{Z,mid,k}M^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{V,mid,i}M^{2i}+C_{\chi,k}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i}M^{2i}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{V,mid,i}M^{2i}+C_{\chi,k}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i}M^{2i}\bigg)(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

(4.96)

\displaystyle+\tilde{C}_{5,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}+C_{V,error}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{4}.

To derive the relationship between
E_{\beta}(\partial_{t}^{k}W^{(\beta)})(T)
and
E_{\beta}(\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)})(T)
. we start with the equation for
W^{(\beta)}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}W^{(\beta)}=\Delta W^{(\beta)}+2(1-\chi)\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta)}-\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}+W^{(\beta)}_{error},

(4.97)

where

\displaystyle W^{(\beta)}_{error}:=

\displaystyle~\big(1-b_{0}^{(\beta)}\big)V_{t}^{(\beta)}-\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}V^{(\beta)}-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}V_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}+F^{(\beta)}

\displaystyle~+\big(b_{0}^{(\beta)}-1\big)Z_{t}^{(\beta)}-\tilde{b}^{(\beta)}Z^{(\beta)}-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\beta)}Z_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}+H^{(\beta)}

(4.98)

\displaystyle~+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\Big(\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}-\delta_{ij}\big)V_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}\Big)_{x_{j}}+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\Big(\big(a_{ij}^{(\beta)}-\delta_{ij}\big)Z_{x_{i}}^{(\beta)}\Big)_{x_{j}}.

Given that (
4.38
) holds for
\alpha=\beta
and recalling the estimates on the coefficients, we apply Lemma
4.3
with
G=W_{error}^{(\beta)}
. This yields: for any
m\leq s-2-k

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}W^{(\beta)}_{error}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}=C_{Werror}M^{m+k+2}(1-\delta)^{\beta}\varepsilon^{2},

(4.99)

for some constant
C_{Werror}
independent of
M
,
\alpha
and
\varepsilon
.

Differentiating (
4.97
) with respect to
t
for
k-2
times, we obtain: when
k\geq 1
is odd,

\partial_{t}^{k}W^{(\beta)}=\Delta^{\frac{k-1}{2}}W_{t}^{(\beta)}+\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{k-1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{k-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2(1-\chi)\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta)}-\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}+W^{(\beta)}_{error}\Big);

(4.100)

when
k\geq 2
is even,

\partial_{t}^{k}W^{(\beta)}=\Delta^{\frac{k}{2}}W^{(\beta)}+\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\partial_{t}^{k-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2(1-\chi)\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta)}-\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}+W^{(\beta)}_{error}\Big).

(4.101)

Noting that for any integer
m\geq 0
,

\Delta^{m}W^{(\beta)}(T)=\Delta^{m}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T),\qquad\Delta^{m}\partial_{t}W^{(\beta)}(T)=\Delta^{m}\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T),

and combining (
4.115
) with
\alpha=\beta+1
and
t=T
, we obtain: when
k\geq 2
is odd,

\begin{split}\partial_{t}^{k}W^{(\beta)}(T)=~&\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)+\sum\limits_{p=0}^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\partial_{t}^{k-2p-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2(1-\chi)\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta)}-\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}+W^{(\beta)}_{error}\Big)\\
&-\sum\limits_{p=0}^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\partial_{t}^{k-2p-2}\Delta^{p}\Big(\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta+1)}+Z^{(\beta+1)}_{error}\Big);\end{split}

(4.102)

when
k\geq 2
is even,

\begin{split}\partial_{t}^{k}W^{(\beta)}=~&\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}+\sum\limits_{p=0}^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\partial_{t}^{k-2p-2}\Delta^{p}\Big(2(1-\chi)\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta)}-\partial_{t}V^{(\beta)}+W^{(\beta)}_{error}\Big)\\
&-\sum\limits_{p=0}^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\partial_{t}^{k-2p-2}\Delta^{p}\Big(\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta+1)}+Z^{(\beta+1)}_{error}\Big).\end{split}

(4.103)

We note that
\chi
is a smooth bounded function so that there exist a sequence constants
C_{1-\chi,p},p=0,1,\cdots,k
such that for any
u\in\mathcal{H}^{k}
,

\sum_{p=0}^{l}\|\Delta^{p}[2(1-\chi)u]\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{1-\chi,l}\sum_{p=0}^{l}\|\Delta^{p}u\|_{L^{2}}=C_{1-\chi,l}\sum_{p=0}^{l}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2p}}.

Combining this with the induction assumption that (
4.38
) and (
4.39
) hold for
\alpha\leq\beta,l\leq s-1
as well as for any
\alpha
and
l\leq k-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}W^{(\beta)}(T)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}W^{(\beta)}(T)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+C_{1-\chi,k-1}\sum\limits_{p=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,p}M^{2p}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{p=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{V,mid,p}M^{2p}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}+kC^{2}_{Werror}M^{2k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{4}

(4.104)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{p=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,p}M^{2p}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{2}+kC^{2}_{Zerror}M^{2k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{4}.

Combining with (
4.94
), and noting the relationship (
4.52
), we obtain

\displaystyle~\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~(1-\delta)^{3}\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta)}(T)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}Z^{(\beta)}(T)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}C_{Z,mid,k}M^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\big(C^{2}_{V,mid,i}+C_{\chi,k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i}\big)M^{2i}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\big(C^{2}_{V,mid,i}+C_{\chi,k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i}\big)M^{2i}\bigg)(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}\Big(\tilde{C}_{V,5,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}+C^{2}_{V,error}\varepsilon\Big)(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{p=0}^{k-1}\Big(C_{1-\chi,k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,p}+C^{2}_{V,mid,p}\Big)M^{2p}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+kC^{2}_{Werror}M^{2k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{4}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{p=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,p}M^{2p}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{2}+kC^{2}_{Zerror}M^{2k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{4}.

Noting that when all constants
C_{V,mid,i},C_{Z,mid,i},i=0,\cdots,s-1
and
\delta,C_{Z_{T},mid,k}
are fixed, we can take
M
large enough such that

\displaystyle M\geq~

\displaystyle\max_{k=0,1,\cdots,s-1}\Bigg\{\frac{10(1-\frac{\delta}{2})}{\delta(1-\delta)^{3}}\cdot\frac{C_{Z,mid,k}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}(C^{2}_{V,mid,i}+C_{\chi,k}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i})}{C_{Z_{T},mid,k}^{2}},

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{10(1-\frac{\delta}{2})}{\delta(1-\delta)^{3}}\cdot\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}(C^{2}_{V,mid,i}+C_{\chi,k}C^{2}_{Z,mid,i})}{C_{Z_{T},mid,k}^{2}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(4.105)

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{10}{\delta(1-\delta)^{2}}\sum\limits_{p=0}^{k-1}(C_{1-\chi,k}C^{2}_{Z,mid,p}+C^{2}_{V,mid,p})\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},\bigg(\frac{10}{\delta}\sum\limits_{p=0}^{k-1}C^{2}_{Z,mid,p}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\},

which together with (
4.74
) implies that

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle(1-\frac{\delta}{2})(1-\delta)^{2\beta}M^{2k}C^{2}_{Z_{T},mid,k}\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{4\delta}{10}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}M^{2k}C^{2}_{Z_{T},mid,k}\varepsilon^{2}

(4.106)

\displaystyle+\frac{2+C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}{2-C_{coe,1}\varepsilon}(\tilde{C}_{V,5,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}+C^{2}_{V,error}\varepsilon)(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{3}

\displaystyle+kC^{2}_{Werror}M^{2k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta-2}\varepsilon^{4}+kC^{2}_{Zerror}M^{2k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{4}.

We now choose
\varepsilon_{4,k}\leq\varepsilon_{3}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}{1-\delta}\tilde{C}_{V,5,k}(1-\delta)^{-2}\varepsilon_{4,k}

\displaystyle+k\Big(\frac{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}{1-\delta}C^{2}_{V,error}+C^{2}_{Werror}\Big)M^{2k}(1-\delta)^{-2}\varepsilon^{2}_{4,k}

(4.107)

\displaystyle+kC^{2}_{Zerror}M^{2k}\varepsilon_{4,k}^{2}\leq\frac{\delta}{10}M^{2k}C^{2}_{Z_{T},mid,k}.

(4.108)

Thus, by setting
\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\varepsilon_{4,k}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{H^{k-1}}^{2}+\big\|Z^{(\beta+1)}(T)\big\|_{H^{k}}^{2}\leq C_{Z_{T},mid,k}^{2}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}M^{2k}\varepsilon^{2}.

(4.109)

Since
l\leq s-1
is finite, taking
\varepsilon_{ZT}=\min\limits_{k=1,\cdots,s-1}\{\varepsilon_{4,k}\}
, we complete the proof of the claim.
∎

Now, similarly to the energy estimate for
\tilde{V}^{(\beta)}(t)
in (
4.88
), we can derive the energy estimate for
\tilde{Z}^{(\beta+1)}(t)=\partial_{t}^{k}z^{(\beta+1)}
as follows:

E_{\beta+1}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta+1)}(t))\leq C_{\tilde{Z}to\tilde{Z}}(\varepsilon)\left(E_{\beta}(\tilde{Z}^{(\beta+1)}(T))+C_{Z,5,k}(1-\delta)^{2\beta}\varepsilon^{3}\right)

(4.110)

where
C_{\tilde{Z}to\tilde{Z}}(\varepsilon)\leq C_{\tilde{Z}to\tilde{Z}}(\varepsilon_{5,k})=\frac{3}{2}<2
is a constant when choosing
\varepsilon_{lem}\leq\varepsilon_{5,k}
and

C_{Z,5,k}
is a constant independent of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
.

Putting the estimate (
4.86
) into (
4.110
), we have
for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{t}^{k+1}Z^{(\beta+1)}(t)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\big\|\partial_{t}^{k}Z^{(\beta+1)}(t)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~
