for
s-1
times, we obtain that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{s+1}v^{(\alpha)}+b^{(\alpha)}_{0}\partial_{t}^{s}v^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b^{(\alpha)}_{i}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}

(4.123)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\chi\cdot\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}_{t}=g^{(\alpha)},

and

\partial_{t}^{s+1}z^{(\alpha)}-b^{(\alpha)}_{0}\partial_{t}^{s}z^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b^{(\alpha)}_{i}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}=h^{(\alpha)},

(4.124)

where

\displaystyle g^{(\alpha)}=

\displaystyle b^{(\alpha)}_{0}\partial_{t}^{s}v^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{s-1}\Big(b_{0}^{(\alpha)}v^{(\alpha)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}v^{(\alpha)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\Big),

and

\displaystyle h^{(\alpha)}=

\displaystyle-b_{0}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s}z^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{s-1}\Big(-b_{0}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}\Big).

The proof of this case is quite similar to the proof of (
4.15
). We only list the key steps here.

1.

The equations for
\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}
and
\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}
.

Owing to our small assumptions on the coefficients, we rewrite the equations for
\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}
and
\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}
as follows:

When
s
is odd,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{s+1}v^{(\alpha)}=\partial_{t}\Delta^{\frac{s}{2}}v^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{s}{2}}\partial_{t}^{s-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2\chi Z^{(\alpha)}_{t}+\partial_{t}v^{(\alpha)}-v_{error}^{(\alpha)}\Big),

(4.125)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{s+1}z^{(\alpha)}=\partial_{t}\Delta^{\frac{s}{2}}z^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{s}{2}}\partial_{t}^{s-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2\chi z^{(\alpha)}_{t}+\partial_{t}v^{(\alpha)}-v_{error}^{(\alpha)}\Big),

(4.126)

when
s
is even,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{s+1}v^{(\alpha)}=\Delta^{\frac{s+1}{2}}v^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{s}{2}}\partial_{t}^{s-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2\chi z^{(\alpha)}_{t}+\partial_{t}v^{(\alpha)}-v_{error}^{(\alpha)}\Big),

(4.127)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{s+1}v^{(\alpha)}=\Delta^{\frac{s+1}{2}}v^{(\alpha)}-\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\frac{s}{2}}\partial_{t}^{s-2l-2}\Delta^{l}\Big(2\chi z^{(\alpha)}_{t}+\partial_{t}v^{(\alpha)}-v_{error}^{(\alpha)}\Big).

(4.128)

Consequently, we can establish the following relationships between
\partial_{t}^{k}v^{(\alpha)}
and
\Delta^{\frac{k}{2}}v^{(\alpha)}
and between
\partial_{t}^{k}z^{(\alpha)}
and
\Delta^{\frac{k}{2}}z^{(\alpha)}
. Specifically, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\Big|\|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{(\alpha)}\|_{L^{2}}-\|v^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}}\Big|+\Big|\|\partial_{t}^{k}z^{(\alpha)}\|_{L^{2}}-\|z^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}}\Big|=O(M^{k}\varepsilon^{2})+O(M^{k-1}\varepsilon).

(4.129)

Recalling the definition
w^{(\alpha)}=v^{(\alpha)}+z^{(\alpha)}
, and combining it with the above relationships, we obtain for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|\|\partial_{t}^{k}w^{(\alpha)}\|_{L^{2}}-\|w^{(\alpha)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}}\big|=O(M^{k-1}\varepsilon)
.

2.

The non-increasing of
E_{\alpha}(z^{(\alpha)})(T)
.

We directly compute the difference
E_{\alpha}(z^{(\alpha)})(T)-E_{\alpha-1}(z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)
and find that

E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)})(T)-E_{\alpha-1}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)=E_{\alpha}(w^{(\alpha-1)})(T)-E_{\alpha-1}(z^{(\alpha-1)})(T).

(4.130)

Combining this with the aforementioned relationships, we deduce that the above expression is equal to

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)-E_{\alpha-1}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)+E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha-1)})(T)

(4.131)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{s}z^{(\alpha-1)}\partial_{t}^{s}v^{(\alpha-1)}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha-1)}{\mathrm{d}}x.

Standard energy estimates yield

E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)-E_{\alpha-1}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)=O(M^{2s}\varepsilon^{3}),

(4.132)

E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha-1)})(T)\leq\frac{3}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\big\|\chi\partial_{t}^{s}z^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t+O(M^{2s-1}\varepsilon^{2}),

(4.133)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{s}z^{(\alpha-1)}\partial_{t}^{s}v^{(\alpha-1)}+\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha-1)}

(4.134)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{T}\big\|\chi\partial_{t}^{s}z^{(\alpha)}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t+O(M^{2s-1}\varepsilon^{2}).

Compared with the coefficients of the equation (
4.124
) for
\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}
with those of the equation (
4.10
) for
Z^{(\alpha)}
, we see that they are identical, with the only difference being in the force term. Consequently, we can apply Theorem
2.8
to the system of
\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}
yielding

E\big(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}(T)\big)\leq D\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\big|\partial_{t}^{s}z^{(\alpha)}\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+O(M^{2s}\varepsilon^{4}).

(4.135)

Combining these results, we arrive at

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)})(T)-E_{\alpha-1}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2D}E_{\alpha-1}(z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)+O(M^{2s}\varepsilon^{3})+O(M^{2s-1}\varepsilon^{2}).

(4.136)

Given the assumption
E_{\alpha-1}(z^{(\alpha-1)})(T)=O(M^{2s}\varepsilon^{2})
, by choosing
M
sufficiently large and
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we can ensure that
E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)})(T)
is non-increasing with respect to
\alpha
.

3.

Uniform Boundedness for
E\big(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}(t)\big)
and
E\big(\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}(t)\big)
.
Returning to the equation for
\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}
, and invoking the well-posedness, for some fixed
T
, we have that
E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)})(t)
is uniformly bounded. Consequently, by (
4.123
),
E_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)})(t)
is also uniformly bounded.

4.

Completion of the Proof.
Utilizing the result from the first step, we establish the uniform boundedness of the time-space norms of
\partial_{t}^{s-1}z^{(\alpha)}(t)
and
\partial_{t}^{s-1}v^{(\alpha)}(t)
. This completes the proof of (
4.39
).

∎

4.3
Uniqueness

In this section, we aim to demonstrate that
y
is the unique solution to System (
4.1
) that satisfies (
1.31
) with some constant
C_{uni}>0
. To proceed, we assume the existence of another solution
\tilde{y}\in\cap_{i=0}^{2}C^{i}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s},s\geq\{n+2,4\}
that also satisfies (
4.1
) and the bound (
1.31
).
For notational simplicity, we define:

\tilde{b}^{v}=\tilde{b}(t,x,v,v_{t},\nabla v),~b_{k}^{v}=v_{k}(t,x,v,v_{t},\nabla v),~a_{ij}^{v}=a_{ij}(t,x,v,v_{t},\nabla v),

for any function
v
and for any
k=0,\cdots,n
and
i,j=1,\cdots,n
.

Let

w=y-\tilde{y}
. Then, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}^{2}w-\Delta w+w_{t}=w_{error},&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&w(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&w(0,x)=0,~w_{t}(0,x)=0,&x\in&~\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.137)

where

\displaystyle w_{error}=~

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{b}^{y}y-\tilde{b}^{\tilde{y}}\tilde{y}\right)+\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left(((a_{ij}^{y}-\delta_{ij})y_{x_{i}})_{x_{j}}-((a_{ij}^{\tilde{y}}-\delta_{ij})\tilde{y}_{x_{i}})_{x_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left((1-b_{0}^{y})y_{t}-(1-b_{0}^{\tilde{y}})\tilde{y}_{t}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(b_{i}^{y}y_{x_{i}}-b_{i}^{\tilde{y}}\tilde{y}_{x_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=:~

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

Next, we expand each
I_{i}
for
i=1,2,3,4
. Stating with
I_{1}
:

I_{1}=\tilde{b}^{y}y-\tilde{b}^{\tilde{y}}\tilde{y}=(\tilde{b}^{y}-\tilde{b}^{\tilde{y}})y+\tilde{b}^{\tilde{y}}(y-\tilde{y}).

(4.138)

The first term on the right-hand side can be written as:

\displaystyle\tilde{b}(t,x,y,y_{t},y_{x_{1}},\cdots,y_{x_{n}})-\tilde{b}(t,x,\tilde{y},\tilde{y}_{t},\tilde{y}_{x_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{y}_{x_{n}})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{b}(y,y_{t},y_{x_{1}},\cdots,y_{x_{n}})-\tilde{b}(\tilde{y},y_{t},y_{x_{1}},\cdots,y_{x_{n}})}{y-\tilde{y}}(y-\tilde{y})

\displaystyle+\frac{\tilde{b}(\tilde{y},y_{t},y_{x_{1}},\cdots,y_{x_{n}})-\tilde{b}(t,x,\tilde{y},\tilde{y}_{t},y_{x_{1}},\cdots,y_{x_{n}})}{y_{t}-\tilde{y}_{t}}(y_{t}-\tilde{y}_{t})

\displaystyle+\frac{\tilde{b}(t,x,\tilde{y},\tilde{y}_{t},y_{x_{1}},\cdots,y_{x_{n}})-\tilde{b}(t,x,\tilde{y},\tilde{y}_{t},\tilde{y}_{x_{1}},\cdots,y_{x_{n}})}{y_{x_{1}}-\tilde{y}_{x_{1}}}(y_{x_{1}}-\tilde{y}_{x_{1}})

\displaystyle+\cdots

\displaystyle+\frac{\tilde{b}(t,x,\tilde{y},\tilde{y}_{t},\tilde{y}_{x_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{y}_{x_{n-1}},y_{x_{n}})-\tilde{b}(t,x,\tilde{y},\tilde{y}_{t},\tilde{y}_{x_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{y}_{x_{n-1}},\tilde{y}_{x_{n}})}{y_{x_{n}}-\tilde{y}_{x_{n}}}(y_{x_{n}}-\tilde{y}_{x_{n}})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{y}w+\tilde{b}_{y_{t}}w_{t}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{b}_{y_{i}}w_{x_{i}}.

Therefore,
I_{1}
is equal to

I_{1}=(y\tilde{b}_{y}+\tilde{b}^{\tilde{y}})w+y\tilde{b}_{y_{t}}w_{t}+y\sum_{k=1}^{n}\tilde{b}_{y_{x_{k}}}w_{x_{k}},

(4.139)

where
\tilde{b}_{y},\tilde{b}_{y_{t}},\tilde{b}_{y_{x_{k}}}
are bounded functions for any
k=1,\cdots,n
.

Similarly, we can expand
I_{2},I_{3}
and
I_{4}
:

\begin{split}I_{2}&=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left(\left(y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y}w+y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y_{t}}w_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y_{x_{k}}}w_{x_{k}}\right)_{x_{j}}+((a_{ij}^{\tilde{y}}-\delta_{ij})w_{x_{i}})_{x_{j}}\right),\\
I_{3}&=y_{t}b_{0,y}w+\left(y_{t}b_{0,y_{t}}+(1-b_{0}^{\tilde{y}})\right)w_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}y_{t}b_{0,y_{x_{k}}}w_{x_{k}},\\
I_{4}&=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{x_{i}}\left(b_{i,y}w+b_{i,y_{t}}w_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{i,y_{x_{k}}}w_{x_{k}}\right)+b_{i}^{\tilde{y}}w_{x_{i}}\right).\end{split}

(4.140)

Here
a_{ij,y},a_{ij,y_{t}},a_{ij,y_{x_{k}}},b_{0,y},b_{0,y_{t}},b_{0,y_{x_{k}}},b_{i,y},b_{i,y_{t}},b_{i,y_{x_{k}}},
are bounded functions for any
i,j,k=1,\cdots,n
.

Now, we proceed to prove that
w
must be zero. We define the energy of the system (
4.137
) as follows:

E(t)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left(|w_{t}|^{2}+|\nabla w|^{2}\right){\mathrm{d}}x.

Next, we multiply (
4.137
) by
w_{t}
and integrate over
\Omega
, yielding:

E(t)+\int_{\Omega}|w_{t}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}w_{error}w_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x.

We need to estimate
\int_{\Omega}w_{error}w_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x
, specifically
\int_{\Omega}I_{i}w_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x
for
i=1,2,3,4
.
Using the expansions (
4.139
) and (
4.140
), along with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain:

\Big|\int_{\Omega}(I_{1}+I_{3}+I_{4})w_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big|\leq C_{1}E(t),

for some constant
C_{1}>0
.

Finally, we need to handle
\int_{\Omega}I_{2}w_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x
, The first two terms of
I_{2}w_{t}
are similar to the previous cases and can be controlled by
E(t)
, that is,

\bigg|\int_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left(y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y}w+y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y_{t}}w_{t}\right)_{x_{j}}\Big)w_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x\bigg|\leq C_{2}E(t).

For the remaining two terms, we can use integration by parts to obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\bigg(w_{t}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y_{x_{k}}}w_{x_{k}}\Big)_{x_{j}}+w_{t}\Big(\big(a_{ij}^{\tilde{y}}-\delta_{ij}\big)w_{x_{i}}\Big)_{x_{j}}\bigg){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k,j=1}^{n}\frac{y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y_{x_{k}}}+y_{x_{i}}a_{ik,y_{x_{j}}}}{2}w_{x_{i}}w_{x_{j}}\bigg)_{t}-\bigg(\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{(a_{ij}^{\tilde{y}}-\delta_{ij})}{2}w_{x_{i}}w_{x_{j}}\bigg)_{t}

\displaystyle+\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k,j=1}^{n}\frac{y_{x_{i}}a_{ij,y_{x_{k}}}+y_{x_{i}}a_{ik,y_{x_{j}}}}{2}\Big)_{t}w_{x_{i}}w_{x_{j}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(a_{ij}^{\tilde{y}})_{t}w_{x_{i}}w_{x_{j}}.

Given our assumption that the solution satisfies the estimate (
1.31
), and considering the boundedness of the coefficient functions
a_{ij,y_{x_{k}}}
, we can conclude that the above terms are bounded by

C_{3}\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}E(t)}{{\mathrm{d}}t}+C_{4}E(t),

for some constants
C_{3},C_{4}>0
. Therefore, we have the following inequality:

(1-C_{3}\varepsilon)\frac{{\mathrm{d}}E}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\leq(C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{4})E(t)

When
\varepsilon
satisfies
0<1-C_{3}\varepsilon<1
, applying Gronwall’s inequality and noting that
E(0)=0
, we conclude that
E(t)\equiv 0
for all
t\geq 0
.
This completes the proof of the uniqueness of the solution.

5
Proof of Theorem
1.3

We consider the local null controllability problem for the fully nonlinear damped wave equations:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &y_{tt}+2y_{t}-\Delta y+y=F(y,y_{t},\nabla y,\nabla^{2}y)+\chi\cdot u,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&y(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&y(0,x)=y^{0},~y_{t}(0,x)=y^{1},&x\in&~\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(5.1)

where
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
satisfies
0\leq\chi(x)\leq 1
,
\chi|_{\omega}\equiv 1
, and
\chi
supports in a neighbourhood of
\omega
, with
\omega\subset\Omega\cap O_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\partial\Omega)
, and

F(\lambda)=O\big(|\lambda|^{2}\big),\quad(\lambda\to 0),

(5.2)

with

\lambda=\Big(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{i}(i=1,\cdots,n),\lambda_{ij}(i,j=1,\cdots,n)\Big).

Before proceeding with the proof, we offer several remarks here.

Remark 5.1
.

Our nonlinear term
F
is independent of
\nabla y_{t}
, this is mainly for simplicity, otherwise we need to deal with terms
v_{tx_{i}}
, and terms
z_{tx_{i}}
in the dual system. But under the assumption of
(T,\omega)
and
\varepsilon\ll 1
, the observability inequality might also be right.

Remark 5.2
.

Recall that in the second section, we let
y=e^{t}\tilde{y}
to reduce a classical linear wave equation to a damped one. Similarly, for a classical nonlinear wave equation, we can use the same method to reduce it to (
5.1
).

We draw attention to the fact that the outcome articulated in Theorem
1.3
is characterized by the conditions:
y_{t}(T)=0,~y_{tt}(T)=0,
as opposed to the conditions:
y(T)=0,~y_{t}(T)=0.
This discrepancy arises from the inherent complexity associated with fully nonlinear equations, which precludes a direct solution approach. To circumvent this, it is necessary to apply differentiation with respect to
t
to the equation, thereby converting it into a quasi-linear form. Consequently, the objective of our control strategy is shifted to target the state variables
(y_{t},y_{tt})
at the terminal time
T
, rather than
(y,y_{t})
. Pursuing control over
(y,y_{t})
may introduce additional layers of complexity. This represents a novel insight that has emerged from our examination of fully nonlinear equations, underscoring the distinctive challenges they present in comparison to their linear counterparts.

Proof..

We first consider (
5.1
) intuitively. Let
v=y_{t}
, we have

-\Delta y+y=F(y,v,\nabla y,\nabla^{2}y)-v_{t}-2v+\chi\cdot u

(5.3)

differentiate (
5.3
) by
t
formally, we get

v_{tt}+b_{0}v_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}v_{x_{i}x_{j}}=\tilde{b}v+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}v_{x_{i}}+\chi\cdot u_{t},

(5.4)

where

\displaystyle a_{ij}=

\displaystyle~\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial F}{\partial y_{x_{i}x_{j}}}(y,v,\nabla y,\nabla^{2}y),

\displaystyle b_{0}=

\displaystyle~2-\frac{\partial F}{\partial v}(y,v,\nabla y,\nabla^{2}y),

\displaystyle b_{i}=

\displaystyle~\frac{\partial F}{\partial y_{x_{i}}}(y,v,\nabla y,\nabla^{2}y),

\displaystyle\tilde{b}=

\displaystyle~\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}(y,v,\nabla y,\nabla^{2}y)-1.

(5.5)

Inspired by (
5.3
) and (
5.4
), we set up the following iteration schemes: taking

(y^{(0)},z^{(0)},v^{(0)})\equiv 0,

knowing
(y^{(\alpha-1)},z^{(\alpha-1)},v^{(\alpha-1)})
, we define
(y^{(\alpha)},z^{(\alpha)},v^{(\alpha)})
as follows

\displaystyle-\Delta y^{(\alpha)}+y^{(\alpha)}=

\displaystyle~F\big(y^{(\alpha-1)},v^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla y^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla^{2}y^{(\alpha-1)}\big)

(5.6)

\displaystyle~-v^{(\alpha-1)}_{t}-2v^{(\alpha-1)}-2\chi\left(z^{(\alpha-1)}(t)-z^{(\alpha-1)}(0)\right),

\displaystyle v^{(\alpha)}_{tt}+b^{(\alpha)}_{0}v^{(\alpha)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}x_{j}}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b^{(\alpha)}_{i}v^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}v^{(\alpha)}-2\chi\cdot z^{(\alpha)}_{t},

(5.7)

and

\displaystyle z^{(\alpha)}_{tt}-b^{(\alpha)}_{0}z^{(\alpha)}_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a^{(\alpha)}_{ij}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}x_{j}}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)}_{x_{i}}+\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}z^{(\alpha)},

(5.8)

with boundary value

\displaystyle y^{(\alpha)}(t,x)=0,~v^{(\alpha)}(t,x)=0,~z^{(\alpha)}(t,x)=0,\quad(t,x)\in(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,

(5.9)

and initial (or final) data

\displaystyle~v^{(\alpha)}(0,x)=y^{1},~v^{(\alpha)}_{t}(0,x)=\Delta y^{0}-y^{0}-2y^{1}+F(y^{0},y^{1},\nabla y^{0},\nabla^{2}y^{0}),

\displaystyle~z^{(\alpha)}(T,x)=v^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x)+z^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x),

(5.10)

\displaystyle~z^{(\alpha)}_{t}(T,x)=v_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x)+z_{t}^{(\alpha-1)}(T,x),

where

\displaystyle a_{ij}^{(\alpha)}=a_{ij}(y^{(\alpha-1)},v^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla y^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla^{2}y^{(\alpha-1)}),\quad i,j=1,\cdots,n

(5.11)

\displaystyle b^{(\alpha)}_{i}=b_{i}(y^{(\alpha-1)},v^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla y^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla^{2}y^{(\alpha-1)}),\quad i=0,\cdots,n

\displaystyle\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}=\tilde{b}^{(\alpha)}(y^{(\alpha-1)},v^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla y^{(\alpha-1)},\nabla^{2}y^{(\alpha-1)}).

By Picard iteration method, we can prove that

\displaystyle\big(v^{(\alpha)},~v^{(\alpha)}_{t}\big)\to(v,~v_{t})\;\;

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\;L^{\infty}\big(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2}\big)\times L^{\infty}\big(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-3}\big),

\displaystyle\big(z^{(\alpha)},~z^{(\alpha)}_{t}\big)\to(z,~z_{t})\;\;

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\;L^{\infty}\big(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2}\big)\times L^{\infty}\big(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-3}\big),

(5.12)

\displaystyle y^{(\alpha)}\to y\;\;

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\;L^{\infty}\big(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-1}\big),

\displaystyle y^{(\alpha)}_{t}\to y_{t}\;\;

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\;L^{\infty}\big(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s-2}\big),

as
\alpha\to\infty
. The proof is similar to (but more complicated than) that of Theorem
1.2
.

The next step is to prove
v=y_{t}
. By (
5.7
) and (
5
), we can check that
v
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{tt}+b_{0}v_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}v_{x_{i}x_{j}}=\tilde{b}v+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}v_{x_{i}}-2\chi\cdot z_{t},&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\Omega,\\
&v(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~(0,T)\times\partial\Omega,\\
&v(0,x)=y^{1},~v(T,x)=0,~v_{t}(T,x)=0,&x\in&~\Omega,\\
&v_{t}(0,x)=-2y^{1}+\Delta y^{0}-y^{0}+F(y^{0},y^{1},\nabla y^{0},\nabla^{2}y^{0}),&x\in&~\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(5.13)

and
y
satisfies

v_{t}+2v-\Delta y+y=F\big(y,v,\nabla y,\nabla^{2}y\big)-2\chi\big(z(t)-z(0)\big).

(5.14)

Denote
\bar{v}=y_{t}
, differentiating (
5.14
) by
t
, we get

v_{tt}+2v_{t}-\Delta\bar{v}+\bar{v}=F_{y}\cdot\bar{v}+F_{v}\cdot v_{t}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}F_{y_{x_{i}}}\bar{v}_{x_{i}}+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}F_{y_{x_{i}x_{j}}}\bar{v}_{x_{i}x_{j}}-2\chi\cdot z_{t},

which can be written as

v_{tt}+b_{0}v_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}\bar{v}_{x_{i}x_{j}}=\tilde{b}\bar{v}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}\bar{v}_{x_{i}}-2\chi\cdot z_{t}.

(5.15)

Subtracting from (
5.13
), we get

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}(v-\bar{v})_{x_{i}x_{j}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}(v-\bar{v})_{x_{i}}+\tilde{b}(v-\bar{v})=0,&x\in&~\Omega,\\
&v-\bar{v}=0,&x\in&~\partial\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

Noting that
a_{ij},~b_{i},~\tilde{b}
are functions of
y
and
v
, this is a linear equation of
v-\bar{v}
. To prove
v=\overline{v}
, we multiply the equation by
-v+\bar{v}
and make an integration by parts, then we get

\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}(v-\bar{v})_{x_{i}}(v-\bar{v})_{x_{j}}{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\big(b_{i}-\partial_{x_{j}}a_{ij}\big)(v-\bar{v})(v-\bar{v})_{x_{i}}+\tilde{b}(v-\bar{v})^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x.

Noting that
|b_{i}-\partial_{j}a_{ij}|+|\tilde{b}+1|+|a_{ij}-\delta_{ij}|=O(\varepsilon)
, we have

\int_{\Omega}\big|\nabla(v-\overline{v})\big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\frac{3C\varepsilon-1}{1-2C\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}|v-\overline{v}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x.

Taking
\varepsilon
small enough such that
C\varepsilon<\frac{1}{3}
, hence we get
v=\bar{v}=y_{t}
, satisfying

y_{t}(T)=0,~y_{tt}(T)=0.

Then

u(t)=-2\chi\left(z(t)-z(0)\right),

is the desired control function.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
2.8

The appendix is devoted to showing the proof of Theorem
2.8
.
Denote

Q^{T}:=(0,T)\times\Omega,\quad\Gamma^{T}:=(0,T)\times\partial\Omega.

Consider the following linear system

\left\{\begin{aligned} &z_{tt}+b_{0}z_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{ij}z_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}z_{x_{k}}+\tilde{b}z=0,&(t,x)\in&~Q^{T},\\
&z(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~\Gamma^{T},\\
&z(0,x)=z_{0},~z_{t}(0,x)=z_{1}&x\in&~\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(A.1)

with

\begin{cases}\|a^{ij}-\delta_{ij}\|_{C^{1}(\overline{Q}^{T})}<\tilde{\varepsilon},i,j=1,\cdots,n\\
\|b_{0}-1\|_{C^{1}(\overline{Q}^{T})}<\tilde{\varepsilon},\|\tilde{b}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{Q}^{T})}<\tilde{\varepsilon}\\
\|b_{k}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{Q}^{T})}<\tilde{\varepsilon},k=1,\cdots,n.\end{cases}

(A.2)

In this section, we will prove Theorem
2.8
, i.e. there exists
\varepsilon_{5}>0
, such that if
\varepsilon_{1}\leq\varepsilon_{5}
, the observability inequality holds for any solution of (
A.1
)

\|z_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|z_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq D\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}|z_{t}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(A.3)

for some constant
D>0
depends on
T,\varepsilon_{5}
,
n
,
\Omega
and
\omega
.

We attempt to apply the methodology presented in
[
27
,
13
]
to construct a proof for inequality (
A.3
). The proof is based on Carleman estimate and primarily divided into two main steps. First, we establish the
H^{1}
-norm Carleman estimate as detailed in Proposition
A.1
. Following this, to derive the observability inequality, it is essential to eliminate the
z^{2}
term appearing on the right-hand side of the inequality. To accomplish this, we proceed to establish an
L^{2}
-norm Carleman estimate.

In order to secure the Carleman estimate for the system described by (
A.1
), we commence by confirming that the assumption (
1.6
) concerning
(T,\omega)
yields the subsequent property. This property is instrumental in laying the groundwork for the derivation of the Carleman estimate in the
H^{1}
norm.

Lemma A.1
.

Assume that
(T,\omega)
satisfies assumption
1.6
. Assume (
A.2
) is valid. Define
\psi(x)=\frac{2\max\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}|x-x_{0}|^{2}}{\min\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}|x-x_{0}|^{2}}|x-x_{0}|^{2}
, there exists a small
\varepsilon_{6}
depends on
\varepsilon_{0}
,
\Omega
and
n
, such that if in
1.6
, we have
\tilde{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon_{6}
, then the following statements are valid.

(a)

There exists a positive constant
\mu_{0}>4
,
such that for any
(t,x,\xi)\in\overline{Q^{T}}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[2a^{jk^{\prime}}(a^{j^{\prime}k}\psi_{x_{j^{\prime}}})_{x_{k^{\prime}}}\right]\xi^{j}\xi^{k}\geq\mu_{0}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}\xi^{j}\xi^{k},

(A.4)

and for any
(t,x)\in\overline{Q^{T}}
,

\frac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}(t,x)\psi_{x_{j}}\psi_{x_{k}}\geq\max\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\psi(x)\geq\min\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\psi(x)\geq 0.

(A.5)

(b)

Let

\Gamma_{t}=\Big\{x\in\partial\Omega:\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}(t,x)\psi_{x_{j}}n^{k}>0\Big\},

(A.6)

and for
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,

O_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\Gamma_{t})=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:d(x,\Gamma_{t})<\varepsilon_{0}\},

we have

\Big(\bigcup_{t\in[0,T]}O_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\Gamma_{t})\Big)\cap~\Omega\subseteq\omega.

(A.7)

Proof of (a).

First, we prove (
A.4
). Since
a^{ij}
satisfies (
A.2
), we have that

\|a^{ij}-\delta_{ij}\|_{C^{1}(\overline{Q}^{T})}<\varepsilon_{1},i,j=1,\cdots,n.

(A.8)

This implies that for any
\mu_{0}

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}\xi^{j}\xi^{k}\leq\mu_{0}\big(1+n^{2}\varepsilon_{1}\big)|\xi|^{2}.

(A.9)

Let
d=\frac{\max\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}|x-x_{0}|^{2}}{\min\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}|x-x_{0}|^{2}}
. Direct computation shows

\displaystyle~\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[2a^{jk^{\prime}}(a^{j^{\prime}k}\psi_{x_{j^{\prime}}})_{x_{k^{\prime}}}\right]\xi^{j}\xi^{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\big[8d|\xi|^{2}+2\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[a^{jk^{\prime}}(a^{j^{\prime}k}\psi_{x_{j^{\prime}}})_{x_{k^{\prime}}}-4d\delta_{jk}\right]\xi^{j}\xi^{k}\big]

(A.10)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~8|\xi|^{2}-2\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\left|\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}a^{jk^{\prime}}(a^{j^{\prime}k}\psi_{x_{j^{\prime}}})_{x_{k^{\prime}}}-4d\delta_{jk}\right||\xi^{j}\xi^{k}|.

We observe that

\displaystyle~\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}a^{jk^{\prime}}\left(a^{j^{\prime}k}\psi_{x_{j^{\prime}}}\right)_{x_{k^{\prime}}}-4d\delta_{jk}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~4d\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}a^{jk^{\prime}}\left(a^{j^{\prime}k}\big(x^{j^{\prime}}-x_{0}^{j^{\prime}}\big)\right)_{x_{k^{\prime}}}-4d\delta_{jk}

(A.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~4d\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}a^{jk^{\prime}}\left(\big(a^{j^{\prime}k}-\delta_{j^{\prime}k}\big)\big(x^{j^{\prime}}-x_{0}^{j^{\prime}}\big)\right)_{x_{k^{\prime}}}+4d\big(a^{jk}-\delta_{jk}\big).

By (
A.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|4d\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}a^{jk^{\prime}}\left(\big(a^{j^{\prime}k}-\delta_{j^{\prime}k}\big)\big(x^{j^{\prime}}-x_{0}^{j^{\prime}}\big)\right)_{x_{k^{\prime}}}+4d\big(a^{jk}-\delta_{jk}\big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle d\Big(n\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\varepsilon}+1)\tilde{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\max_{x\in\bar{\Omega}}\{\psi(x)\}}+4n(1+\tilde{\varepsilon})\tilde{\varepsilon}+4\tilde{\varepsilon}\Big).

(A.12)

Substituting this into (
A
), we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[2a^{jk^{\prime}}(a^{j^{\prime}k}\psi_{x_{j^{\prime}}})_{x_{k^{\prime}}}\right]\xi^{j}\xi^{k}\geq(8-C(n,\tilde{\varepsilon},\psi)\tilde{\varepsilon})|\xi|^{2},

(A.13)

where

C(n,\tilde{\varepsilon},\psi)=d\big(n^{2}\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\varepsilon}+1)\max_{x\in\bar{\Omega}}\{\psi(x)\}+4n^{2}(1+\tilde{\varepsilon})+4n\big)
.

Combining (
A.9
) and (
A.13
), we know that by choosing
\varepsilon_{6}
small enough such that

C(n,\varepsilon_{6},\psi)\varepsilon_{6}<4

(A.14)

then we have (
A.4
) for some
\mu_{0}>4
.

For the proof of (
A.5
), it suffices to check the first part of the inequality. We compute

\frac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}(t,x)\psi_{x_{j}}\psi_{x_{k}}=4d^{2}|x-x_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}(t,x)-\delta^{jk}\big)\psi_{x_{j}}\psi_{x_{k}},

(A.15)

which implies that

\frac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}(t,x)\psi_{x_{j}}\psi_{x_{k}}\geq 4d^{2}|x-x_{0}|^{2}-nd^{2}\tilde{\varepsilon}|x-x_{0}|^{2}.

(A.16)

Choosing
\varepsilon_{6}<\frac{2}{n}
and satisfies (
A.14
), then
this implies that

\frac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}(t,x)\psi_{x_{j}}\psi_{x_{k}}\geq 2d^{2}|x-x_{0}|^{2}\geq 2\max\lim\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}|x-x_{0}|^{2}.

(A.17)

Thus, (
A.5
) is valid for some small
\varepsilon_{6}>0

∎

Proof of (b).

It suffices to demonstrate that for
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\bigcup_{t\in[0,T]}O_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\Gamma_{t})\subseteq O_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\Gamma).

(A.18)

Utilizing the definition of
\Gamma_{t}
and
\psi
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}=

\displaystyle~\Big\{x\in\partial\Omega:\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}(t,x)\psi_{x_{j}}n^{k}>0\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Big\{x\in\partial\Omega:(x-x_{0})\cdot\bm{\nu}>\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(\delta_{jk}-a^{jk}(t,x)\big)\psi_{x_{j}}n^{k}\Big\}

(A.19)

Recalling (
A.8
), it follows that

|\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(\delta_{jk}-a^{jk}(t,x)\big)\psi_{x_{j}}n^{k}|\leq\tilde{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}\max_{x\in\bar{\Omega}}\{\psi(x)\}}

(A.20)

Thus, we obtain

\Gamma_{t}\subseteq~\Big\{x\in\partial\Omega:(x-x_{0})\cdot\bm{\nu}>-C(n,\psi)\varepsilon_{1}\Big\},

(A.21)

which holds for all
~t\in[0,T]
. Consequently, by selecting
\varepsilon_{6}
sufficiently small such that
C(n,\psi)\varepsilon_{6}<\varepsilon_{0}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\bigcup_{t\in[0,T]}\Gamma_{t}\subseteq\Gamma,

(A.22)

and (
A.7
) is satisfied. This completes the proof.
∎

We now state a Carleman estimate in the
H^{1}
-norm. Denote

\left\{\begin{aligned} &v(t,x)=\theta z,~\theta(t,x)=e^{l(t,x)},~l(t,x)=\lambda\phi(t,x),\\
&\phi(t,x)=\psi(x)-c_{1}(t-T/2)^{2},c_{1}\in(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

(A.23)

Proposition A.1
.

Assuming that
(T,\omega)
satisfies the condition given in Assumption
1.6
and that (
A.2
) holds with
\tilde{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon_{6}
in Lemma
A.1
. Then there exists a constant
\lambda_{0}>0
such that for all
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
, any
z\in H^{1}_{0}(Q^{T})
fulfills the following internal Carleman estimate

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}\Big(\lambda(z_{t}^{2}+|\nabla z|^{2})+\lambda^{3}z^{2}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg(\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}|z_{tt}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{ij}z_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}(z_{t}^{2}+\lambda^{2}z^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\bigg).

Remark A.1
.

Let

T_{1}=\max\left\{2\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}~,~1+25s_{0}(n+2)\sqrt{n}\right\},

(A.25)

where

\kappa_{1}=\max\limits_{t\in[0,T],x\in\overline{\Omega}}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}\psi_{x_{j}}\psi_{x_{k}},\quad s_{0}=\max\limits_{t\in[0,T],x\in\partial\Omega}\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}\psi_{x_{j}}n^{k}.

Direct computation shows that if
(T,\omega)
satisfies Assumption
1.6
, then
T>T_{1}
.

The proof of this Lemma can follow the procedures outlined in
[
12
, Chapter 4]
, and thus we do not provide a detailed proof here.

As mentioned at the beginning of this section, in order to obtain (
A.3
), we need to eliminate the
z^{2}
terms on the right-hand side of (
A.24
). Following the approach in
[
13
]
, we need consider the
L^{2}
-norm Carleman estimate for the following system:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &z_{tt}+b_{0}z_{t}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(a^{ij}z_{x_{i}}\big)_{x_{j}}+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}z_{x_{k}}+\tilde{b}z=F,&(t,x)\in&~Q^{T},\\
&z(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~\Gamma^{T},\end{aligned}\right.

(A.26)

where
F\in L^{1}(0,T;H^{-1}(\Omega))
and
a^{ij},b_{0},b_{i},\tilde{b},i,j=1,2,\cdots,n
satisfies (
A.2
).

The
L^{2}
estimate requires consideration of the weak solution to system (
A.26
):

Definition A.1
.

A function
z\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega)
is called a weak solution to (
A.26
) if

\bigg(z~,~\eta_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\eta_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}\bigg)_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}=\int_{0}^{T}\langle f(t,\cdot),\eta(t,\cdot)\rangle_{H^{-1}(\Omega),H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)}{\mathrm{d}}t,

(A.27)

holds for any
\eta\in H_{0}^{2}\big(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega)\cap H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)\big)
.

Note that there are no initial data in (
A.26
), so we need the following lemma for weak solutions.

Lemma A.2
.

Given
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<T
and
g\in L^{2}((t_{1},t_{2})\times\Omega)
. Assume that
z\in L^{2}(Q^{T})
is a weak solution to (
A.26
) with
z=g
in
(t_{1},t_{2})\times\Omega
. Assume that (
A.2
) is valid. Then there exists a small constant
\varepsilon_{7}
, such that if
\tilde{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon_{7}
in (
A.2
), then we have

z\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))\cap C^{1}([0,T];H^{-1}(\Omega)),

and there exists a constant
C=C(T,t_{1},t_{2},\Omega,\tilde{\varepsilon})>0
, such that

\|z\|_{C([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))\cap C^{1}([0,T];H^{-1}(\Omega))}\leq C\big(\|f\|_{L^{1}(0,T;H^{-1}(\Omega))}+\|g\|_{L^{2}((t_{1},t_{2})\times\Omega)}\big).

(A.28)

The proof of this lemma differs from that of
[
30
, Lemma 5.1]
solely in that the coefficients are time-dependent.
This results in additional terms appearing during the regularization process of the solution with respect to time. Nonetheless, by leveraging the smallness assumption (
A.2
), we can achieve the desired conclusion.

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

Fix arbitrary

t_{i},i=3,4
satisfying

t_{1}<t_{3}<t_{4}<t_{2}.

(A.29)

For any
\delta\in(0,\min(t_{3}-t_{1},t_{2}-t_{4}))
, we have for any
t,x\in(t_{3},t_{6})\times\Omega
,

z^{\delta}:=(z*\rho_{\delta})(t,x)=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}z(s,x)\rho_{\delta}(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s,

(A.30)

where
\rho_{\delta}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is a Friedrichs mollifier.

According to equation (
A.26
), we can verify that
z^{\delta}\in C^{\infty}([t_{3},t_{4}];L^{2})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} &z^{\delta}_{tt}+z^{\delta}_{t}-\Delta z^{\delta}=F_{1}^{\delta}+F_{2}^{\delta},&(t,x)\in&~Q^{T},\\
&z^{\delta}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~\Gamma^{T},\end{aligned}\right.

(A.31)

where
F^{\delta}_{1}=F*\rho_{\delta}
and

\displaystyle F_{2}^{\delta}=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(b_{0}(t)-b_{0}(s))z_{t}(s,x)\rho_{\delta}(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{n}\big((a^{ij}(s,x)-a^{ij}(t,x))z_{x_{i}}(s,x)\big)_{x_{j}}\rho_{\delta}(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle+\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(b_{k}(t)-b_{k}(s))z_{x_{k}}(s,x)\rho_{\delta}(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(\tilde{b}(t)-\tilde{b}(s))z(s,x)\rho_{\delta}(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s.

Since for any
t\in(t_{3},t_{4})
,
\rho_{\delta}(t-s)
has compact support. Then we can use integration by parts in the sense of distributions, to deduce that

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(b_{0}(t)-b_{0}(s))z_{t}(s,x)\rho_{\delta}(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}z(s,x)\partial_{s}\big((b_{0}(t)-b_{0}(s))\rho_{\delta}(t-s)\big){\mathrm{d}}s.

(A.32)

Denote
(-\Delta)^{-1}
as the inverse of the Laplacian operator
-\Delta
with Dirichlet boundary conditions. Thus, for any

u,v\in L^{2}(\overline{\Omega}),a\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}),b,c\in C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
, if
(-\Delta)^{-1}v=v=u=0
on
\partial\Omega
, then

\begin{split}((au_{x_{i}})_{x_{j}},(\Delta)^{-1}v)_{H^{-2},H^{2}}\leq~&C_{1}\|a\|_{C^{1}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{L^{2}},\\
((bu_{x_{i}}),(\Delta)^{-1}v)_{H^{-2},H^{2}}\leq~&C_{2}\|b\|_{C^{0}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{H^{-1}},\\
((cu),(\Delta)^{-1}v)_{H^{-2},H^{2}}\leq~&C_{3}\|c\|_{C^{0}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{H^{-2}}\leq C_{4}\|c\|_{C^{0}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

(A.33)

where
C_{i},i=1,2,3,4
depend only on
\Omega
and
n
.
Multiplying the equation for

z^{\delta}

in system (
A.31
) by
\Delta^{-1}z^{\delta}
, integrating over
\Omega
, and using (
A.2
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\|z^{\delta}\|_{C^{0}(t_{3},t_{4};L^{2})\cap C^{1}(t_{3},t_{4};H^{-1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\tilde{C}\big(\|F_{1}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(t_{3},t_{4};L^{2})}\|z^{\delta}\|_{C^{0}(t_{3},t_{4};L^{2})}+\tilde{\varepsilon}\|z\|_{L^{2}(t_{1},t_{2};L^{2})}\|z^{\delta}\|_{C^{0}(t_{3},t_{4};L^{2})\cap C^{1}(t_{3},t_{4};H^{-1})}\big).

(A.34)

Here
\tilde{C}>0
is a constant independent with
\delta,z,z^{\delta},F^{\delta}_{1}
and
\tilde{\varepsilon}
.

Thus, together with the assumption that
z=g
in
(t_{1},t_{2})\times\Omega
, it immediately implies that

\|z^{\delta}\|_{C^{0}(t_{3},t_{4};L^{2})\cap C^{1}(t_{3},t_{4};H^{-1})}^{2}\leq\tilde{C}_{1}\big(\|F_{1}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(t_{3},t_{4};L^{2})}^{2}+\|g\|_{L^{2}(t_{1},t_{2};L^{2})}^{2}\big).

(A.35)

Letting
\delta
tends to zero and using the properties of the Friedrichs mollifier
\rho_{\delta}
, we can conclude that
z\in C([t_{3},t_{6}];L^{2}(\Omega))\cap C^{1}([t_{3},t_{6}];H^{-1}(\Omega))
and

\|z\|_{C^{0}(t_{3},t_{6};L^{2})\cap C^{1}(t_{3},t_{6};H^{-1})}^{2}\leq\tilde{C}_{1}\big(\|F\|_{L^{2}(t_{1},t_{2};L^{2})}^{2}+\|g\|_{L^{2}(t_{1},t_{2};L^{2})}^{2}\big).

(A.36)

Since (
A.26
) is a linear system, using the well-posedness theory of linear wave equations, we can get (
A.28
). Therefore, we complete the proof.
∎

Our Carleman estimate for the above hyperbolic operators in
L^{2}
-norm is as follows.

Proposition A.2
.

Assuming that
(T,\omega)
satisfies the condition given in Assumption
1.6
and that (
A.2
) holds with
\tilde{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon_{6}
in Lemma
A.1
. Let
T_{1}
be given in (
A.25
). Then there exists a constant
\lambda_{0}^{*}>0
such that for
\forall~T>T_{1}
and
\lambda>\lambda_{0}^{*}
, and every solution
z\in C^{0}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))
satisfying
z(0,x)=z(T,x)=0,~x\in\Omega
and

z_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}\in H^{-1}\big(Q^{T}\big),

it holds

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.37)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg(\Big\|\theta\Big(z_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}+2z_{t}+z\Big)\Big\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\bigg).

We will first assume Proposition
A.2
and then provide the proof for Theorem
2.8
. The proof for Proposition
A.2
will be presented later.

Proof of Theorem
2.8
.

For any
(z_{0},z_{1})\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)\times L^{2}(\Omega)
, the system (
A.1
) admits a unique solution

z\in C([0,T];H_{0}^{1}(\Omega))\cap C^{1}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega)).

Define the energy of the system by

\mathcal{E}(t)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\Big[|z_{t}(t)|^{2}+\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}(t)z_{x_{k}}(t)+|z(t)|^{2}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x.

Multiplying the system (
A.1
) by
z_{t}
, integrating it on
\Omega
, and using integration by parts, we get

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(t)+2\int_{\Omega}b_{0}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x=-\int_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}z_{t}z_{x_{k}}+\tilde{b}zz_{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x\geq-C\varepsilon\mathcal{E}(t).

(A.38)

Since that

\int_{\Omega}b_{0}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq(2+C\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}(t),

we have

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(t)+(2+C\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}(t)=e^{-(2+C\varepsilon)t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\big(e^{(2+C\varepsilon)t}\mathcal{E}(t)\big)\geq 0.

Integrating the above inequality on
(0,T)
, we get

e^{(2+C\varepsilon)T}\mathcal{E}(T)\geq\mathcal{E}(0).

(A.39)

Step 1.
We put

\tilde{T}_{j}=\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{j}\Big)T,\quad\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{j}=\Big(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{j}\Big)T,\quad j=0,1

for constants
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}<\frac{1}{2}
.

Then we choose a nonnegative cut-off function
\tilde{\zeta}\in C_{0}^{2}([0,T])
such that

\tilde{\zeta}(t)\equiv 1,\qquad\forall~t\in[\tilde{T}_{1},\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{1}].

(A.40)

Set
\tilde{z}(t,x)=\tilde{\zeta}(t)z_{t}(t,x)
for
(t,x)\in Q^{T}
. Then
\tilde{z}
solves

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{z}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}(a^{jk}\tilde{z}_{x_{j}})_{x_{k}}+2\tilde{z}_{t}+\tilde{z}=\tilde{\zeta}_{tt}z_{t}+\tilde{\zeta}_{t}z_{t}+2\tilde{\zeta}_{t}z_{tt}+\tilde{\zeta}(2-b_{0})z_{tt}&&\\
&+\tilde{\zeta}\bigg[\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}(a^{jk}_{t}z_{x_{j}})_{x_{k}}-\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(b_{k}z_{x_{k}})_{t}-(\tilde{b}-1+\partial_{t}b_{0})z_{t}-\tilde{b}_{t}z\bigg],&(t,x)\in&~Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{z}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~\Gamma_{T},\\
&\tilde{z}(0,x)=\tilde{z}(T,x)=0,&x\in&~\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

(A.41)

Let
T_{1}
and
\phi
be given by (
A.25
) and (
A.23
). Then by Proposition
A.2
, there exists
\lambda_{0}^{*}>0
such that for all
T>T_{1}
and
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}^{*}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}\tilde{z}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg(\Big\|\theta\big(\tilde{\zeta}_{tt}z_{t}+\tilde{\zeta}_{t}z_{t}+2\tilde{\zeta}_{t}z_{tt}+\tilde{\zeta}(2-b_{0})z_{tt}\big)\Big\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}\tilde{z}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bigg\|\theta\tilde{\zeta}\Big[\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}(a^{jk}_{t}z_{x_{j}})_{x_{k}}-\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(b_{k}z_{x_{k}})_{t}-(\tilde{b}-1+\partial_{t}b_{0})z_{t}-\tilde{b}_{t}z\Big]\bigg\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}^{2}\bigg).

Using H
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
lder inequality and Sobolev embedding theorem, we find that

\left\{\begin{aligned} \big\|\theta(\tilde{\zeta}_{tt}+\tilde{\zeta}_{t})z_{t}\big\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}&\leq C\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}\\
\big\|2\theta\tilde{\zeta}_{t}z_{tt}\big\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}&\leq C(1+\lambda)\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}\\
\big\|\theta\tilde{\zeta}(2-b_{0})z_{tt}\big\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}&\leq C(1+\lambda)\varepsilon\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})},\end{aligned}\right.

(A.43)

where
\tilde{Q}=\big((0,\tilde{T}_{1})\cup(\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{1},T)\big)\times\Omega
, and

\displaystyle\bigg\|\theta\tilde{\zeta}\Big[\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}(a^{jk}_{t}z_{x_{j}})_{x_{k}}-\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(b_{k}z_{x_{k}})_{t}-(\tilde{b}-1+\partial_{t}b_{0})z_{t}-\tilde{b}_{t}z\Big]\bigg\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}

(A.44)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1+\lambda)\varepsilon\big(\|\theta\nabla z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}+\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}\big).

Combining (
A.41
)–(
A.44
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda\|\theta\tilde{z}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}

(A.45)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda^{2}\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}^{2}+C\lambda^{2}\|\theta\tilde{z}\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\omega)}^{2}+C\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\Big(\|\theta\nabla z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda^{2}\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}^{2}+C\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\Big(\|\theta\nabla z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}\Big).

On the other hand, by (
A.40
), we find that

\|\theta\tilde{z}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}\geq\int_{\tilde{T}_{1}}^{\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\int_{\Omega}\theta^{2}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Thus we have

\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}\leq\|\theta\tilde{z}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}^{2}.

(A.46)

It follows from (
A.45
) and (
A.46
) that

\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}\leq C\lambda\Big(\|\theta z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\theta\nabla z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\Big).

(A.47)

Step 2.
We set

R_{0}=\min\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\sqrt{\psi(x)},\quad R_{1}=\max\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\sqrt{\psi(x)}.

By the definition (
A.23
) of the function
\phi
, we can see there exists an
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
, such that

\phi(t,x)\leq\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}-\frac{c_{1}T^{2}}{8}<0,\quad\forall~(t,x)\in\tilde{Q}.

(A.48)

Further, since that

\phi\Big(\frac{T}{2},x\Big)=\psi(x)\geq R_{0}^{2},\quad\forall~x\in\Omega,

one can find an
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1/2)
, such that

\phi(t,x)\geq\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{2},\quad\forall~(t,x)\in\big(\tilde{T}_{0},\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{0}\big)\times\Omega:=Q_{0}.

(A.49)

Combining (
A.47
)–(
A.49
), we obtain that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda R_{0}^{2}}\|z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\Big(e^{\lambda(R_{1}^{2}-cT^{2}/4)}\|z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}e^{2\lambda R_{1}^{2}}\|\nabla z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}+e^{2\lambda R_{1}^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\Big).

Noting that

\|z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{Q})}^{2}+\|\nabla z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}\leq 2T\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq 2Te^{(1+\varepsilon)T}\mathcal{E}(T),

hence we have

\|z_{t}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{0})}^{2}\leq C\lambda\Big(e^{\lambda(R_{1}^{2}-R_{0}^{2}-cT^{2}/4)}\mathcal{E}(T)+e^{2\lambda R_{1}^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\Big).

(A.50)

Step 3.
We choose a nonnegative function
\zeta\in C^{1}([\tilde{T}_{0},\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{0}])
with
\zeta(\tilde{T}_{0})=\zeta(\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{0})=0
. Multiplying the equation in (
A.1
) by
\zeta z
, integrating it in
Q_{0}
and using integration by parts, we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{0}}\zeta\Big(z_{t}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}z_{x_{k}}+z^{2}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=2\int_{\tilde{T}_{0}}^{\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{0}}\zeta(t)\mathcal{E}(t){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{Q_{0}}\zeta z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{Q_{0}}\zeta_{t}zz_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{Q_{0}}\zeta z\Big(b_{0}z_{t}+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}z_{x_{k}}+(\tilde{b}-1)z\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{Q_{0}}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C\varepsilon\int_{Q_{0}}\zeta|\nabla z|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{Q_{0}}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C\varepsilon\int_{\tilde{T}_{0}}^{\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{0}}\zeta(t)\mathcal{E}(t){\mathrm{d}}t.

Thus we obtain

\min\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq C\int_{Q_{0}}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(A.51)

Note that by (
A.38
), we also have

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(t)+\int_{\Omega}b_{0}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x=-\int_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}z_{t}z_{x_{k}}+\tilde{b}zz_{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x\leq C\varepsilon\mathcal{E}(t),

hence we get

\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\big(e^{-C\varepsilon t}\mathcal{E}(t)\big)\leq-e^{-C\varepsilon t}\int_{\Omega}b_{0}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq 0.

Then we have

\mathcal{E}(t)\geq e^{-C\varepsilon t}\mathcal{E}(t)\geq e^{-C\varepsilon T}\mathcal{E}(T),\qquad\forall~t\in[0,T].

(A.52)

Combining (
A.51
) and (
A.52
), we have

\mathcal{E}(T)\leq C\int_{Q_{0}}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(A.53)

It follows from (
A.50
) and (
A.53
) that

\mathcal{E}(T)\leq C\lambda\Big(e^{\lambda(R_{1}^{2}-R_{0}^{2}-cT^{2}/4)}\mathcal{E}(T)+e^{2\lambda R_{1}^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\Big).

(A.54)

Noting that
R_{1}^{2}-R_{0}^{2}-cT^{2}/4<0
, let
\lambda
be large enough such that

C\lambda e^{\lambda(R_{1}^{2}-R_{0}^{2}-cT^{2}/4)}\leq\frac{1}{2},

then can deduce from (
A.54
) that

\mathcal{E}(T)\leq C_{1}e^{C_{1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(A.55)

where
C_{1}
is a positive constant independent of initial data.

Combining (
A.55
) and (
A.39
), we obtain

\|z_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|z_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{E}(0)\leq C_{2}e^{C_{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}z_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

with a constant
C_{2}>0
independent of initial data. Thus we obtain the desired inequality.
∎

A.1
Carleman estimate in
L^{2}
-norm

This subsection is devoted to prove Proposition
A.2
.

Throughout this subsection, we fix the function
\phi
in (
A.23
), a parameter
\lambda>0
, and a function
z\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))
holding
z(0,x)=z(T,x)=0
for
x\in\Omega
. For any
K>1
, we choose a function
\rho(x)\in C^{2}(\overline{\Omega})
with
\min\limits_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\rho(x)=1
so that

\rho(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} 1,&~x\in\omega,\\
K,&~d(x,\omega)\geq\frac{1}{\ln K},\end{aligned}\right.

(A.56)

For any integer
m\geq 3
, let
h=\frac{T}{m}
. Define

z_{m}^{i}=z_{m}^{i}(x)=z(ih,x),~\phi_{m}^{i}=\phi_{m}^{i}(x)=\phi(ih,x),\quad i=0,1,\cdots,m.

(A.57)

and

a^{jk}_{i}=a^{jk}_{i}(x)=a^{jk}(ih,x),\quad i=0,1,\cdots,m;~j,k=1,\cdots,n.

(A.58)

Let
\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in(H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)\times(L^{2}(\Omega))^{3})^{m+1}
satisfy the following system:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{w_{m}^{i+1}-2w_{m}^{i}+w_{m}^{i-1}}{h^{2}}-\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{n}\partial_{j_{2}}(a^{j_{1},j_{2}}_{i}\partial_{j_{1}}w_{m}^{i})\\
=&\frac{r_{1m}^{i+1}-r^{i}_{1m}}{h}+r_{2m}^{i}+\lambda z_{m}^{i}e^{2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}+r_{m}^{i},\quad 1\leq i\leq m-1,~x\in\Omega,\\
&w_{m}^{i}=0,\qquad 0\leq i\leq m,~x\in\partial\Omega,\\
&w_{m}^{0}=w_{m}^{m}=r_{2m}^{0}=r_{2m}^{m}=r_{m}^{0}=r_{m}^{m}=0,~r_{1m}^{0}=r_{1m}^{1},\quad x\in\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

(A.59)

The set of admissible sequences for (
A.59
) is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{ad}:=\Big\{

\displaystyle\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in(H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)\times(L^{2}(\Omega))^{3})^{m+1}\Big|

(A.60)

\displaystyle\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}~\text{satisfy}~\eqref{system:one}\Big\}.

Note that we can easily see the set
\mathcal{A}_{ad}\not=\emptyset
because
\big\{(0,0,0,-\lambda z_{m}^{i}e^{2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}})\big\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}
.

Now, let us introduce the cost functional

\displaystyle J\Big(\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\Big)=\frac{h}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho\frac{|r_{1m}^{m}|}{\lambda^{2}}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{m}}{\mathrm{d}}x

(A.61)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{h}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\left[\int_{\Omega}|w_{m}^{i}|^{2}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{\Omega}\rho\left(\frac{|r_{1m}^{i}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{|r_{2m}^{i}|^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}\right)e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}+K\int_{\Omega}|r_{m}^{i}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\right].

Let us consider the following optimal problem:

\inf_{\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}}J(\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m})=d.

(A.62)

We have the following key proposition.

Proposition A.3
.

For any
K>1
and
m\geq 3
, problem (
A.62
) admits a unique solution
\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}
, such that

J\Big(\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\Big)=\min_{\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}}J\Big(\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\Big).

Furthermore, for

p_{m}^{i}=p_{m}^{i}(x):=K\hat{r}_{m}^{i}(x),\qquad 0\leq i\leq m,

(A.63)

one has

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{m}^{0}=\hat{w}_{m}^{m}=p_{m}^{0}=p_{m}^{m}=0,\qquad x\in\Omega,

(A.64)

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{m}^{i},p_{m}^{i}\in H^{2}(\Omega)\cap H_{0}^{1}(\Omega),\quad 1\leq i\leq m-1

and the following optimality conditions:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{p_{m}^{i}-p_{m}^{i-1}}{h}+\rho\frac{\hat{r}_{1m}^{i}}{\lambda^{2}}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}=0,\\
&p_{m}^{i}-\rho\frac{\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}}{\lambda^{4}}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}=0,\end{aligned}\right.\qquad 1\leq i\leq m,~x\in\Omega

(A.65)

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{p_{m}^{i}-2p_{m}^{i-1}+p_{m}^{i-1}}{h^{2}}-\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{n}\partial_{j_{2}}(a^{j_{1},j_{2}}_{i}\partial_{j_{1}}p_{m}^{i})\\
&+\hat{z}_{m}^{i}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}=0,~x\in\Omega\\
&p_{m}^{i}=0,\qquad x\in\partial\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.\quad 1\leq i\leq m-1.

(A.66)

Moreover, there is a constant
C=C(K,\lambda)>0
, independent of
m
, such that

h\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\int_{\Omega}\Big[|\hat{w}_{m}^{i}|^{2}+|\hat{r}_{1m}^{i}|^{2}+|\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}|^{2}+K|\hat{r}_{m}^{i}|^{2}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x+h\int_{\Omega}|\hat{r}_{1m}^{m}|^{2}\leq C

(A.67)

and

\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\int_{\Omega}\Big[\frac{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i+1}-\hat{w}_{m}^{i})^{2}}{h^{2}}+\frac{(\hat{r}_{1m}^{i+1}-\hat{r}_{1m}^{i})^{2}}{h^{2}}+\frac{(\hat{r}_{2m}^{i+1}-\hat{r}_{2m}^{i})^{2}}{h^{2}}+K\frac{(\hat{r}_{m}^{i+1}-\hat{r}_{m}^{i})^{2}}{h^{2}}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\frac{C}{h}.

(A.68)

Remark A.2
.

For any
\big\{(w_{m}^{i},r_{1m}^{i},r_{2m}^{i}),r_{m}^{i}\big\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}
, since
(a^{j_{1},j_{2}}_{i})
is positive definite, by standard regularity results of elliptic equations, we obtain
w_{m}^{i}\in H^{2}(\Omega)\cap H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
.

Proof..

The proof is divided into several steps.

Step 1. Existence and uniqueness of
\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}
.

Let
\{\{(w_{m}^{i,j},r_{1m}^{i,j},r_{2m}^{i,j}),r_{m}^{i,j}\}_{i=0}^{m}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathcal{A}_{ad}
be a minimizing sequence of
J
. Due to the coercivity of
J
and noting that
w_{m}^{i,j}
solves an elliptic equation, it can be shown that

\{\{(w_{m}^{i,j},r_{1m}^{i,j},r_{2m}^{i,j}),r_{m}^{i,j}\}_{i=0}^{m}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}

is bounded in
\mathcal{A}_{ad}
. Therefore, there exists a subsequence of
\{\{(w_{m}^{i,j},r_{1m}^{i,j},r_{2m}^{i,j}),r_{m}^{i,j}\}_{i=0}^{m}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
converging weakly to some

\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in(H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)\times(L^{2}(\Omega))^{3})^{m+1}.

Note that the constraint condition (
A.59
) is a linear system, we obtain

\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}.

and
\hat{w}_{m}^{0}=\hat{w}_{m}^{m}=p_{m}^{0}=p_{m}^{m}=0,~x\in\Omega
.

Since
J
is strictly convex, this optimal target is the unique solution of (
A.62
).

Step 2. The proof of (
A.65
) and (
A.66
).

Fix any

\delta_{0m}^{i}\in H^{2}\cap H_{0}^{1},~\delta_{1m}^{i}\in L^{2},~\delta_{2m}^{i}\in L^{2},\quad i=0,1,\cdots,m

with
\delta_{0m}^{0}=\delta_{0m}^{m}=\delta_{2m}^{0}=\delta_{2m}^{m}=0
and
\delta_{1m}^{0}=\delta_{1m}^{1}
in
\Omega
. For
(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we denote

\left\{\begin{aligned} &r_{m}^{i}:=\frac{\hat{w}_{m}^{i+1}-2\hat{w}_{m}^{i}+\hat{w}_{m}^{i-1}}{h^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0m}^{i+1}-2\delta_{0m}^{i}+\delta_{0m}^{i-1}}{h^{2}}\lambda_{0}\\
&-\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{n}\partial_{j_{2}}\big(a^{j_{1},j_{2}}_{i}\partial_{j_{1}}(\hat{w}_{m}^{i}+\lambda_{0}\delta_{0m}^{i})\big)-\frac{\hat{r}_{1m}^{i+1}-\hat{r}^{i}_{1m}}{h}\\
&-\frac{\delta_{1m}^{i+1}-\delta^{i}_{1m}}{h}\lambda_{1}-\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}-\lambda_{2}\delta_{2m}^{i}-\lambda z_{m}^{i}e^{2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}},~1\leq i\leq m-1,\\
&r_{m}^{0}=r_{m}^{m}=0\end{aligned}\right.

(A.69)

Then we have

\big\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i}+\lambda_{0}\delta_{0m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i}+\lambda_{1}\delta_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i})+\lambda_{2}\delta_{2m}^{i},r_{m}^{i}\big\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}.

Define a function
g
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
by

g(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})=J\Big(\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i}+\lambda_{0}\delta_{0m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i}+\lambda_{1}\delta_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i})+\lambda_{2}\delta_{2m}^{i},r_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\Big).

(A.70)

Since
\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}
is minimum point of
J
,
g
has a minimum at
(0,0,0)
. Hence we have
\nabla g(0,0,0)=0
.

By
\frac{\partial g(0,0,0)}{\partial\lambda_{1}}=\frac{\partial g(0,0,0)}{\partial\lambda_{2}}=0
, and the fact that
\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{ad}
satisfy (
A.59
), one gets

-K\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\int_{\Omega}\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\frac{\delta_{1m}^{i+1}-\delta_{1m}^{i}}{h}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\int_{\Omega}\rho\frac{\hat{r}_{1m}^{i}\delta_{1m}^{i}}{\lambda^{2}}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}{\mathrm{d}}x=0,

(A.71)

-K\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\int_{\Omega}\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\delta_{2m}^{i}{\mathrm{d}}x+\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\int_{\Omega}\rho\frac{\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}\delta_{2m}^{i}}{\lambda^{4}}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}{\mathrm{d}}x=0,

(A.72)

combined with (
A.59
) and
p_{m}^{0}=p_{m}^{m}=\hat{r}_{2m}^{m}=0
in
\Omega
gives (
A.65
). From
\frac{g(0,0,0)}{\partial\lambda_{0}}=0
, one obtains

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\int_{\Omega}\bigg\{K\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\Big[\frac{\delta_{0m}^{i+1}-2\delta_{0m}^{i}+\delta_{0m}^{i-1}}{h^{2}}-

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{n}\partial_{j_{2}}\big(a^{j_{1},j_{2}}_{i}+

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{m}^{i}\delta_{0m}^{i}e^{-2\lambda\phi_{m}^{i}}\bigg\}{\mathrm{d}}x=0

(A.73)

together with
p_{m}^{0}=p_{m}^{m}=\delta_{0m}^{0}=\delta_{0m}^{m}=0
in
\Omega
, implies that
p_{m}^{i}=K\hat{r}_{m}^{i}
is a weak solution of (
A.66
). By the regularity theory for elliptic equations, one sees that
\hat{w}^{i}_{m},p_{m}^{i}\in H^{2}\cap H_{0}^{1}
for
1\leq i\leq m-1
.

Step 3. The proof of (
A.67
) and (
A.68
).

The proof of the above estimates are similar to those of
[
13
]
, so we omit the details, then we complete the proof.
∎

Now we are in a position to prove Propostion
A.2
.

Proof of Propostion
A.2
.

The main idea is to choose a special
\eta
, so that

\eta_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\eta_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}=\lambda ze^{2\lambda\phi}+\cdots,

where we get the desired term
\lambda\|\theta z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}
and reduce the estimate to that for
\|\eta\|_{H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})}
. The proof is divided into several steps.

Step 1.
Firstly, recall the functions
\{(\hat{w}_{m}^{i},\hat{r}_{1m}^{i},\hat{r}_{2m}^{i}),\hat{r}_{m}^{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}
in Proposition
A.3
, put

\left\{\begin{aligned} \tilde{w}^{m}(t,x)&=\frac{1}{h}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m-1}\Big((t-ih)\hat{w}^{i+1}_{m}(x)-(t-(i+1)h)\hat{w}^{i}_{m}(x)\Big)\chi_{(ih,(i+1)h]}(t),\\
\tilde{r}_{1}^{m}(t,x)&=\hat{r}^{0}_{1m}(x)\chi_{\{0\}}(t)\\
&+\frac{1}{h}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m-1}\Big((t-ih)\hat{r}^{i+1}_{1m}(x)-(t-(i+1)h)\hat{r}^{i}_{1m}(x)\Big)\chi_{(ih,(i+1)h]}(t),\\
\tilde{r}_{2}^{m}(t,x)&=\frac{1}{h}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m-1}\Big((t-ih)\hat{r}^{i+1}_{2m}(x)-(t-(i+1)h)\hat{r}^{i}_{2m}(x)\Big)\chi_{(ih,(i+1)h]}(t),\\
\tilde{r}^{m}(t,x)&=\frac{1}{h}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m-1}\Big((t-ih)\hat{r}^{i+1}_{m}(x)-(t-(i+1)h)\hat{r}^{i}_{m}(x)\Big)\chi_{(ih,(i+1)h]}(t),\end{aligned}\right.

By (
A.67
) and (
A.68
), there exist a subsequence of
\{(\tilde{w}^{m},\tilde{r}_{1}^{m},\tilde{r}_{2}^{m}),\tilde{r}^{m}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}
which converges weakly to some
(\tilde{w},\tilde{r}_{1},\tilde{r}_{2}),\tilde{r}\in H^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
as
m\to\infty
.

Let
\tilde{p}=K\tilde{r}
for some sufficiently large constant
K>1
. By (
A.59
), (
A.65
)–(
A.68
) and Lemma
A.2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{w},~\tilde{p}\in C([0,T];H^{1}_{0}(\Omega))\cap C^{1}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega)),

(A.74)

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{w}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\tilde{w}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}=\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{2}+\lambda\theta^{2}z+\tilde{r},&(t,x)~\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}+\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}=0,&(t,x)~\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}_{t}+\rho\theta^{-2}\frac{\tilde{r}_{1}}{\lambda^{2}}=0,&(t,x)~\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}-\rho\theta^{-2}\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}}{\lambda^{4}}=0,&(t,x)~\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}(t,x)=\tilde{w}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)~\in~&\Gamma^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}(0,x)=\tilde{p}(T,x)=\tilde{w}(0,x)=\tilde{w}(T,x)=0,&x~\in~&\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

(A.75)

Step 2.
Applying Theorem
A.1
to
\tilde{p}
in (
A.75
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}(\lambda^{2}\tilde{p}^{2}+\tilde{p}_{t}^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.76)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big[\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}(\lambda^{2}\tilde{p}^{2}+\tilde{p}_{t}^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big[\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\Big].

Here and hence forth,
C
is a constant independent of
K
and
\lambda
.

By (
A.75
), we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{p}_{ttt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\tilde{p}_{tx_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}+(\theta^{-2}\tilde{w})_{t}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}=0,&(t,x)~\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}_{tt}+\frac{\rho}{\lambda}\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}}{\lambda}-2\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}\Big)=0,&(t,x)~\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}_{t}-\frac{\rho}{\lambda^{2}}\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}}{\lambda^{2}}-\frac{2}{\lambda}\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}\Big)=0,&(t,x)~\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}_{t}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)~\in~&\Gamma^{T},\end{aligned}\right.

(A.77)

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{p}_{tt}-\Delta\tilde{p}=\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big((a^{jk}-\delta_{jk})\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}-\theta^{-2}\tilde{w},&(t,x)\in&~Q^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in&~\Gamma^{T},\\
&\tilde{p}(0,x)=\tilde{p}(T,x)=0,&x\in&~\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

(A.78)

Applying Theorem
A.1
to
\tilde{p}_{t}
in (
A.77
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}(\lambda^{2}\tilde{p}_{t}^{2}+\tilde{p}_{tt}^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C\bigg[\big\|\theta(\theta^{-2}\tilde{w})_{t}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\Big\|\theta\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}(\lambda^{2}\tilde{p}_{t}^{2}+\tilde{p}_{tt}^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C\bigg[\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}(\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\tilde{w}^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\Big\|\theta\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}^{2}

(A.79)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}|^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}+\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\bigg].

Here we note that, in view of (
A.74
) and (
A.78
), we have
\tilde{p}_{t}\in H^{1}(Q^{T})
, hence we can apply Theorem
A.1
to
\tilde{p}_{t}
.

Recalling the smallness assumption (
A.2
) on
a^{ij}
, we have

\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}\Big|\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}\Big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C\varepsilon\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}\big(|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}\tilde{p}|^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(A.80)

Taking
L^{2}
inner product of (
A.78
) with
-\Delta\tilde{p}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}|\nabla^{2}\tilde{p}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{Q^{T}}|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\big(|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}\tilde{p}|^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}|\tilde{w}||\Delta\tilde{p}|{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\big(|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}\tilde{p}|^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{Q^{T}}\big(\theta^{-4}\tilde{w}^{2}+|\Delta\tilde{p}|^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Noting that
e^{C_{1}\lambda}\leq\theta\leq e^{C_{2}\lambda}
for some
C_{1}<C_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}|\nabla^{2}\tilde{p}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq

\displaystyle e^{C\lambda}\int_{Q^{T}}|\nabla^{2}\tilde{p}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.81)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{C\lambda}\int_{Q^{T}}\big(\varepsilon|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}+\theta^{-4}\tilde{w}^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{C\lambda}\int_{Q^{T}}\Big(\varepsilon\theta^{2}|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}+\theta^{2}|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}+\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

By (
A.80
) and (
A.81
), we obtain

\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}\Big|\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}\Big|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C\varepsilon e^{C\lambda}\int_{Q^{T}}\Big[\theta^{2}\big(|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}\big)+\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(A.82)

Step 3.
Noting that by (
A.75
),

-\int_{Q^{T}}(\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{2})\tilde{p}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{Q^{T}}(\tilde{r}_{1}\tilde{p}_{t}-\tilde{r}_{2}\tilde{p}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=-\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Thus we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\Big(\tilde{w}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\tilde{w}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}-\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}-\tilde{r}_{2}-\lambda\theta^{2}z-\tilde{r},~\tilde{p}\Big)_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}

\displaystyle=~-\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z\tilde{p}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-K\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Hence we get

\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+K\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=-\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z\tilde{p}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and (
A.76
), we obtain

\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+K\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(A.83)

Step 4.
Using (
A.75
) and (
A.77
), by the fact that
\tilde{p}_{tt}(0)=\tilde{p}_{tt}(T)=0
in
\Omega
, we get

\displaystyle 0=~

\displaystyle\Big(\tilde{w}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\tilde{w}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}-\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}-\tilde{r}_{2}-\lambda\theta^{2}z-\tilde{r},~\tilde{p}_{tt}\Big)_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\Big(\tilde{w}~,\tilde{p}_{tttt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\tilde{p}_{ttx_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}\Big)_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}

\displaystyle-\int_{Q^{T}}(\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{2})\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.84)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{w}(\theta^{-2}\tilde{w})_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{w}\big(2a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{tx_{j}}+a^{jk}_{tt}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle-\int_{Q^{T}}(\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{2})\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Now we should deal with the terms on the right hand side.

Firstly, it’s easy to see that

\displaystyle-\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{w}(\theta^{-2}\tilde{w})_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{Q^{T}}\Big[\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}-(\theta^{-2})_{tt}\frac{\tilde{w}^{2}}{2}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.85)

\displaystyle=\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\big(\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda\phi_{tt}\tilde{w}^{2}-2\lambda^{2}\phi_{t}^{2}\tilde{w}^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Secondly, by (
A.77
) we have

\displaystyle-\int_{Q^{T}}(\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{2})\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{Q^{T}}(\tilde{p}_{t}\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}-\tilde{p}_{tt}\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.86)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\bigg[\frac{\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}}{\lambda}\Big(\frac{\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}}{\lambda}-2\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}\Big)+\frac{\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\Big(\frac{\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}}{\lambda^{2}}-\frac{2}{\lambda}\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}\Big)\bigg]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}|^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}-\frac{2}{\lambda}\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}-\frac{2}{\lambda^{3}}\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Moreover, by
\tilde{p}=K\tilde{r}
and integration by parts, one gets that

-\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=K\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.87)

and

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{w}\big(2a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{tx_{j}}+a^{jk}_{tt}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{w}_{x_{k}}\big(2a^{jk}_{t}\tilde{p}_{tx_{j}}+a^{jk}_{tt}\tilde{p}_{x_{j}}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.88)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\varepsilon\int_{Q^{T}}\Big[\theta^{2}(|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2})+\theta^{-2}|\nabla\tilde{w}|^{2}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Combining (
A.1
)–(
A.1
), we end up with

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}|^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}-\frac{2}{\lambda}\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}-\frac{2}{\lambda^{3}}\phi_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.89)

\displaystyle+K\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\big(\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda\phi_{tt}\tilde{w}^{2}-2\lambda^{2}\phi_{t}^{2}\tilde{w}^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z\tilde{p}_{tt}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C\varepsilon\int_{Q^{T}}\Big[\theta^{2}(|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2})+\theta^{-2}|\nabla\tilde{w}|^{2}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

By (
A.89
)+
C\lambda^{2}\cdot
(
A.83
) with a sufficiently large
C>0
, using Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, noting (
A.76
), (
A.1
) and (
A.82
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}(\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\tilde{w}^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}|^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}+\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.90)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C\varepsilon e^{C\lambda}\int_{Q^{T}}\Big[\theta^{2}\big(|\nabla\tilde{p}|^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{p}_{t}|^{2}\big)+\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}^{2}\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}|\nabla\tilde{w}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Step 5.
It follows from (
A.75
) that

\displaystyle\Big(\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{2}+\lambda\theta^{2}z+\tilde{r},\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}\Big)_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}

(A.91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\tilde{w}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\tilde{w}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}},\theta^{-2}\tilde{w}\Big)_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{Q^{T}}\tilde{w}_{t}(\theta^{-2}\tilde{w})_{t}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\int_{Q^{T}}a^{jk}\tilde{w}_{x_{j}}(\theta^{-2}\tilde{w})_{x_{k}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\big(\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda\phi_{tt}\tilde{w}^{2}-2\lambda^{2}\phi_{t}^{2}\tilde{w}^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}a^{jk}\tilde{w}_{x_{j}}\tilde{w}_{x_{k}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle-2\lambda\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}a^{jk}\tilde{w}_{x_{j}}\tilde{w}\phi_{x_{k}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

thus we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}|\nabla\tilde{w}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.92)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{Q^{T}}\Big[\theta^{-2}|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{2}+\tilde{r}||\tilde{w}|+\lambda|z\tilde{w}|+\theta^{-2}(\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\tilde{w}^{2})\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{Q^{T}}\Big[\theta^{2}z^{2}+\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\tilde{r}^{2}+\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\tilde{w}^{2}\Big)\Big]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Now we combine (
A.83
), (
A.90
) and (
A.92
), and choose the constant
K
in (
A.83
) so that

K\geq Ce^{2\lambda\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(Q^{T})}}

to absorb the term
C\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}\tilde{r}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t
in (
A.92
). Noting that
\rho(x)\geq 1
and
\varepsilon
can be so small that
C\varepsilon e^{C\lambda}\ll 1
for given
\lambda
, we finally deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{-2}(|\nabla\tilde{w}|^{2}+\tilde{w}_{t}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\tilde{w}^{2}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(A.93)

\displaystyle+\int_{Q^{T}}\rho\theta^{-2}\Big(\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{1}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{r}_{2}|^{2}}{\lambda^{4}}+\tilde{r}_{1}^{2}+\frac{\tilde{r}_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Step 6.
Recall that
(\tilde{w},\tilde{r}_{1},\tilde{r}_{2},\tilde{r})
depends on
K
, so we can denote it by

(\tilde{w}^{K},\tilde{r}_{1}^{K},\tilde{r}_{2}^{K},\tilde{r}^{K}).

Fix
\lambda
and let
K\to\infty
, since
\rho=\rho^{K}(x)\to\infty
for
x\notin\omega
, we can see from (
A.83
) and (
A.93
) that there exists a subsequence of
(\tilde{w}^{K},\tilde{r}_{1}^{K},\tilde{r}_{2}^{K},\tilde{r}^{K})
which converges weakly to some
(\check{w},\check{r}_{1},\check{r}_{2},0)
in

H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})\times(H^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))^{2}\times L^{2}(Q^{T}),

with
\text{supp}\,\check{r}_{j}\subseteq[0,T]\times\overline{\omega},~j=1,2.
By (
A.75
) we see that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\check{w}_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}\check{w}_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}=\partial_{t}\check{r}_{1}+\check{r}_{2}+\lambda\theta^{2}z,&(t,x)\in~&Q^{T},\\
&\check{w}(0,x)=\check{w}(T,x)=0,&x\in~&\Omega,\\
&\check{w}(t,x)=0,&(t,x)\in~&\Gamma_{T}.\end{aligned}\right.

Using (
A.93
) again, we find that

\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{-2}\big(|\partial_{t}\check{r}_{1}|^{2}+\check{r}_{2}^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(A.94)

Then we take the
\eta
in (
A.27
) to be the above
\check{w}
, and find that

\Big(\check{w},\partial_{t}\check{r}_{1}+\check{r}_{2}+\lambda\theta^{2}z\Big)_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}=\Big\langle z_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}},\check{w}\Big\rangle_{H^{-1}(Q^{T}),H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{Q^{T}}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=

\displaystyle~\Big\langle z_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}+2z_{t}+z,\check{w}\Big\rangle_{H^{-1}(Q^{T}),H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})}

\displaystyle+2(z,\check{w}_{t})_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}-(z,\check{w})_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}-(z,\partial_{t}\check{r}_{1}+\check{r}_{2})_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\omega)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\Big\|\theta\Big(z_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}+2z_{t}+z\Big)\Big\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})}

\displaystyle+\|\theta z\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}\big(\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}_{t}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}+\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}\big)

(A.95)

\displaystyle+\|\theta z\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\omega)}\big\|\theta^{-1}(\partial_{t}\check{r}_{1}+\check{r}_{2})\big\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\omega)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sqrt{J}\Big[\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})}+\lambda\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}+\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}_{t}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{-1}\big\|\theta^{-1}(\partial_{t}\check{r}_{1}+\check{r}_{2})\big\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\omega)}\Big],

where

J:=\Big\|\theta\Big(z_{tt}-\sum\limits_{j,k=1}^{n}\big(a^{jk}z_{x_{j}}\big)_{x_{k}}+2z_{t}+z\Big)\Big\|_{H^{-1}(Q^{T})}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\omega}\theta^{2}z^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

is exactly the right hand side of (
A.37
). Since that

\theta^{-1}\check{w}_{t}=\big(\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big)_{t}-\big(\theta^{-1}\big)_{t}\check{w}=\big(\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big)_{t}+\lambda\phi_{t}\check{w},

we have

\displaystyle\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}_{t}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{H^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\lambda\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}\big)

(A.96)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{H_{0}^{1}(Q^{T})}+\lambda\big\|\theta^{-1}\check{w}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q^{T})}\big).

Finally, by (
A.94
)–(
A.96
), we obtain the desired estimate (
A.37
). This completes the proof of Proposition
A.2
.
∎
